      process? / uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok l%shdj,shl (Software development process) msßlaiqï (Testing)
      meje;aùfï mámdáh jvd;a fyd¢ka úia;r flfrkafka my; oelafjk ljrla u.ska o?

          1) Acceptance testing, integrated testing, unit testing
             m%;s.%yK msßlaiqu, wkql,s; msßlaiqu, tall msßlaiqu
          2) Integrated testing, acceptance testing, unit testing
             wkql,s; msßlaiqu, m%;s.%yK msßlaiqu, tall msßlaiqu
          3) Integrated testing, unit testing, acceptance testing.
             wkql,s; msßlaiqu, tall msßlaiqu, ms<s.ekqï msßlaiqu
          4) Unit testing, acceptance testing, integrated testing.
             tall msßlaiqu, m%;s.%yK msßlaiqu, wkql,s; msßlaiqu
          5) Unit testing, integrated testing, acceptance testing.
             tall msßlaiqu, wkql,s; msßlaiqu, m%;s.%yK msßlaiqu

          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

          Option/ úl,amh 1)
          This order is completely reversed. Acceptance testing is usually the final phase, not the first. Unit testing
          must occur early, so this order is incorrect.
          fuu wkqms<sfj, iïmQ¾Kfhkau wdmiq yrjd we;' m%;s.%yK mÍlaIKh idudkHfhka m<uq wÈhr
          fkdj wjidk wÈhrhs' tAll mÍlaIdj l,ska isÿ úh hq;=h" tneúka fuu wkqms<sfj, jerÈhs'

          Option/ úl,amh 2)

          components are verified. Starting with integration or acceptance testing skips essential foundational
          checks. / jerÈhs' fuu wkqms<sfj, o ;d¾lsl fkdfõ' tAll mÍlaIdj hkq ;ks ixrpl i;Hdmkh lrk
          ,o mÍlaIKj, m<uq wÈhrhs' tAldnoaO mÍlaId lsÍu fyda m%;s.%yK mÍlaIKfhka wdrïN lsÍu
          w;HjYH uQ,sl mÍlaIdjka uÕ yßhs'

          Option/ úl,amh 3)
          Incorrect. This order is out of sequence. Integration testing comes after unit testing to ensure combined
          modules work. So this order places unit testing too late.
          jerÈhs' fuu wkqms<sfj, wkqms<sfj,ska neyerhs' tAldnoaO fudähq, l%shd lrk nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd
          tAll mÍlaIKfhka miqj tAldnoaO mÍlaIKh meñfKa' tneúka fuu wkqms<sfj, tAll mÍlaIdj m%udo
          jeähs'

          Option/ úl,amh 4)
          Incorrect. Although this starts correctly with unit testing, it wrongly puts acceptance testing before
          integration. Acceptance testing should come after all components are integrated and tested together.
          jerÈhs' fuh tAll mÍlaIdj iuÕ ksjerÈj wdrïN jqjo" th tAldnoaO lsÍug fmr m%;s.%yK
          mÍlaIKh jerÈ f,i ;nhs' ish¨u ixrpl tAldnoaO lr tlg mÍlaId lsÍfuka miqj m%;s.%yK
          mÍlaIKh meñKsh hq;=h'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    This is the correct and standard order followed in most software development lifecycles
    fndfyda uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%j, wkq.ukh lrk ksjerÈ iy iïu; wkqms<sfj, fuhhs

    •    Unit testing checks individual components or modules.
         tAll mÍlaIdj ;ks ixrpl fyda fudähq, mÍlaId lrhs'

 2. Which of the following is a classification of systems?
    my; oelafjk tAjd w;=frka moaO;s j¾.SlrKhla jkafka l=ula o?

    5)   Structured and hybrid /

    Answer : 3 )

    In system theory, systems are often classified based on how they interact with their environment. The
    most common classification is:
    moaO;s kHdh ;=<" moaO;s fndfyda úg j¾.SlrKh lrkq ,nkafka tAjd tAjdfha mßirh iuÕ wka;¾
    l%shd lrk wdldrh wkqj h' jvd;a fmdÿ j¾.SlrKh jkafka(

    Open System / újD; moaO;sh

    tys ndysr mßirh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrhs

    Receives input and provides output.
    wdodkh ,ndf.k m%;sodkh imhhs

    Example: A business organization that responds to customer feedback
    WodyrKh( mdßfNda.sl m%;sfmdaIKj,g m%;spdr olajk jHdmdßl ixúOdkhla

    Closed System / ixjD; moaO;sh

    Operates in isolation.
    yqol,dj l%shd;aul fõ

    Example: A chemical reaction in a sealed container
    WodyrKh( uqød ;enQ Ndckhl ridhksl m%;sl%shdjla

 3. Consider the following items:
    my; i|yka whs;u i,lkak(

    A. Object oriented / jia;= keUqre
    B. Rapid Application Development / YS>% fhÿï ixj¾Okh
    C. Spiral / i¾ms,dldr
    D. Structured / jHqy.;
    E. Waterfall / Èhwe,s

    “……, …… and …… are system development methodologies”.
    “……, …… iy …… hkq moaO;s ixj¾Ok l%ufõo fõ'”

    Which of the above are most appropriate respectively to fill the blanks in the above statement?
    fuu m%ldYfha ysia;eka msrùu i|yd jvd;au iqÿiq jkafka by; oelafjk ljr tajd o?

    1) A, B and C only.                  2) A, C and D only.                 3) B, C and D only.
       A, B yd C muKs.                      A, C yd D muKs.                     B, C yd D muKs.
    4) B, C and E only.                  5) B, D and E only.
       B, C yd E muKs.                      B, D yd E muKs.
                                                                                                               ( 2011 - 35 )
    Answer : 4 )
    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    Refers to a paradigm or modeling technique (object-oriented analysis and design), and is sometimes
    presented as a methodology, but more often as a design paradigm rather than a structured methodology
    itself. / Paradigm fyda wdlD;s ks¾udK Ys,amSh l%uhla ^jia;=-keUqre úYaf,aIKh iy ks¾udKh& fj;
    fhduq jk w;r" iuyr úg l%ufõohla f,i bÈßm;a lrkq ,efí" kuq;a fndfyda úg jHqy.;
    l%ufõohlg jvd ks¾udK wdo¾Yhla f,i Ndú; fõ'
    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    A recognized software/system development methodology that emphasizes quick prototyping and iterative
    delivery. / blauka uQ,dlD;slrKh iy mqkrdj¾;k fnodyeÍu wjOdrKh lrk ms<s.;a uDÿldx.$moaO;s
    ixj¾Ok l%ufõohls'
    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    A widely acknowledged software/system development methodology that focuses on iterative development
    and risk management. / mqkrdj¾;k ixj¾Okh iy wjodkï l<ukdlrKh flfrys wjOdkh fhduq
    lrk mq¿,a f,i ms<s.;a uDÿldx.$moaO;s ixj¾Ok l%ufõohls'
    Statement / m%ldYh D)
    Refers to structured methodologies, which are formal, systematic approaches to systems development
    (such as Structured Systems Analysis and Design Methodology, SSADM); this is also widely considered
    a classic system development methodology. / moaO;s ixj¾Okh i|yd úêu;a" l%udkql+, m%fõYhka jk
    jHqy.; l%ufõohka fj; fhduq fõ ^jHqy.; moaO;s úYaf,aIKh iy ie,iqï l%ufõoh" (SSADM
    jeks); fuh iïNdjH moaO;s ixj¾Ok l%ufõohla f,i o mq¿,a f,i ie,fla'
    Statement / m%ldYh E)
    One of the most traditional and well-established system/software development methodologies.
    jvd;a idïm%odhsl iy fyd¢ka ia:dms; moaO;s$uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok l%ufõoj,ska tlls'
    Thus, the correct and most widely recognized combination is B (Rapid Application Development), C
    (Spiral), and E (Waterfall)
    fï wkqj" ksjerÈ iy jvd;a mq¿,a f,i ms<s.;a ixfhdackh jkafka B (iS>% fhÿï ixj¾Okh)" C
    ^i¾ms,dldr& iy E ^Èh we,a,& h'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 4) B, C and E only.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4) B, C iy E muKs'

                              2012 SYSTEM MCQ MARKING

   1. Human blood circulatory system can be classified as a
      ukqIH reêr ixirK moaO;sh j¾.SlrKh l< yelafla

          1)    natural and closed system / iajdNdúl iy ixjD; moaO;shla f,i h'
          2)    artificial and closed system / lD;%su iy ixjD; moaO;shla f,i h'
          3)    artificial and open system / lD;%su iy újD; moaO;shla f,i h'
          4)    natural and open system / iajdNdúl iy újD; moaO;shla f,i h'
          5)    natural and artificial system / iajdNdúl iy lD;%su moaO;shla f,i h'

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2012 - 10 )
          Answer : 1 )
          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
          Option/ úl,amh 1)
          The human circulatory system is naturally occurring and closed, meaning blood flows through vessels
          without leaving them.
          ñksia reêr ixirK moaO;sh iajNdúlj isÿjk w;r jid we;" tkï reêrh reêr kd, yryd .,d
          hkafka tAjdhska msgj fkdhEfuks'

          Option/ úl,amh 2)
          Incorrect because the human circulatory system is not man-made.
          jerÈhs" ukao ñksia reêr ixirK moaO;sh ñksid úiska idok ,o tlla fkdjk neúks'

          Option/ úl,amh 3)
          Incorrect; it's not artificial, and blood does not leave vessels as in an open system.
          jerÈhs; th lD;su fkdjk w;r reêrh újD; moaO;shl fuka reêr kd, j,ska msgjkafka ke;'

          Option/ úl,amh 4)
          Incorrect because although natural, the human system is closed, not open.
          jerÈhs" ukao iajdNdúl jqjo" ñksia moaO;sh jid we;" újD; fkdfõ'
          Option/ úl,amh 5)
          Illogical combination: it cannot be both natural and artificial.
          ;d¾lsl fkdjk ixfhdackh( th iajdNdúl yd lD;su hk folu úh fkdyel'
         Therefore, option 1) is the correct answer.
         tneúka" 1) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

   2. Which of the following best describes a non-functional requirement of a mobile phone?
      cx.u ÿrl:khl ld¾h noaO fkdjk (non-functional) wjYH;djla fyd¢kau úia;r flfrkafka my;
      i|yka ljrla u.ska o?

          1)    Sending a short message / flá mKsúvhla heùu
          2)    Receiving a telephone call / ÿrl:k weu;=ula ,eîu
          3)    Selecting a number from the contact list / iïnkaO whf.a f,aLkfhka wxlhla f;dard.ekSu
          4)    Making a telephone call / ÿrl:k weu;=ula ,nd§u
          5)    Having one year warranty for the battery / negßh i|yd wjqreoaol j.lSï ld,hla ;sîu
                                                                                                                                                         ( 2012 - 11 )

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -6-                                           #ictfromabc

    Answer : 5 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    This is a functional requirement because it defines a specific action the mobile phone must perform.
    Sending a short message (SMS) is one of the core features expected from a phone, and it involves a defined
    input (text), process (transmission), and output (delivery). Functional requirements like this describe what
    the system should do, and this action directly relates to the phone's basic communication functionality.
    fuh ld¾hnoaO wjYH;djhls" ukao th cx.u ÿrl:kh úiska bgq l< hq;= ksYaÑ; l%shdjla ks¾jpkh
    lrhs' flá mKsúvhla heùu SMS ÿrl:khlska wfmalaId lrk uQ,sl úfYaIdx.j,ska tlla jk w;r
    thg w¾: olajd we;s wdodkh ^fm<&" l%shdj,sh ^iïfm%aIKh& iy m%;sodkh ^fnod yeÍu& we;=<;a fõ'
    fujeks ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d moaO;sh l< hq;= foa úia;r lrk w;r" fuu l%shdj ÿrl:kfha uQ,sl
    ikaksfõok l%shdldß;ajhg iDcqju iïnkaO fõ'
    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    This also qualifies as a functional requirement, as it refers to the system’s ability to detect, process, and
    notify the user of an incoming call. It is a fundamental function of any mobile phone and involves software
    and hardware interaction to complete the process. Since this describes what the phone does rather than
    how it behaves under certain conditions, it clearly fits the definition of a functional requirement.
    fuh ld¾hnoaO wjYH;djhla f,io iqÿiqlï ,nhs" ukao th meñfKk weu;=ula y÷kd .ekSug"
    ieliSug iy mßYS,lhdg oekqï §ug moaO;sfha yelshdj .ek i|yka lrhs' th ´kEu cx.u
    ÿrl:khl uQ,sl ld¾hhla jk w;r l%shdj,sh iïmQ¾K lsÍu i|yd uDÿldx. iy oDvdx. wka;¾l%shd
    we;=<;a fõ' fuh hï hï ;;ajhka hgf;a th yeisfrk wdldrh fjkqjg ÿrl:kh lrk foa úia;r
    lrk neúka" th ld¾hnoaO wjYH;djhl w¾: oelaùug meyeÈ,sj .e,fma'
    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    This is a functional requirement because it defines how the user interacts with the system’s interface to
    perform a task—selecting a stored contact number. It involves navigating the contact list, searching or
    scrolling, and selecting an entry, all of which are part of the phone's operational behavior. As it describes
    a specific user action and system response, it is considered a functional requirement.
    fuh ld¾hnoaO wjYH;djhls" ukao th mßYS,lhd ld¾hhla bgq lsÍu i|yd moaO;sfha w;=reuqyqK;
    iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrk wdldrh ks¾jpkh lrhs - .nvd l< iïnkaO;d wxlhla f;dard .ekSu' thg
    iïnkaO;d ,ehsia;=j ießieÍu" fiùu fyda wkqp,kh lsÍu iy we;=<;a lsÍula f;dard .ekSu we;=<;a
    jk w;r" tA ish,a, ÿrl:kfha fufyhqï yeisÍfï fldgils' th ksYaÑ; mßYS,l l%shdjla iy moaO;s
    m%;spdrhla úia;r lrk neúka" th ld¾hnoaO wjYH;djhla f,i ie,fla'
    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Making a call is one of the most fundamental functional requirements of a mobile phone. It describes the
    action of initiating a call, connecting to another device through the network, and maintaining
    communication. This behavior is central to what a phone is expected to do and clearly falls under the
    category of functions the system must support.
    weu;=ula .ekSu cx.u ÿrl:khl uQ,slu ld¾hnoaO wjYH;djhkaf.ka tlls' weu;=ula wdrïN lsÍu"
    cd,h yryd fjk;a Wmdx.hlg iïnkaO ùu iy ikaksfõokh mj;ajd .ekSu hk l%shdj th úia;r
    lrhs' fuu yeisÍu ÿrl:khla wfmalaId lrk foag flakaøSh jk w;r moaO;sh iydh Èh hq;= ld¾hhka
    ldKavhg meyeÈ,sju wh;a fõ'
    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    This is a non-functional requirement because it does not define a task the phone performs, but rather a
    support or quality attribute related to the product. A warranty is part of the manufacturer’s commitment to
    quality and reliability, not the phone’s operational capability. It describes the conditions under which the
    battery will be repaired or replaced, which is a business-related specification, not a software or hardware
    function.
    fuh ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;djhls" ukao th ÿrl:kh bgq lrk ld¾hhla ks¾jpkh fkdlrk
    kuq;a ksIamdokhg wod< iydhla fyda .=Kd;aul .=Kdx.hla ks¾jpkh lrhs' j.lSula hkq ÿrl:kfha

    th negßh w¨;ajeähd lrk fyda m%;sia:dmkh lrk fldkafoais úia;r lrhs" th jHdmdrhg wod<
    msßú;rhls" uDÿldx. fyda oDvdx. ld¾hhls'
    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 5) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 3. An electronic washing machine can be best considered as a/an
    bf,lafg%dksl fiaÿï hka;%hla (electronic washing machine) jvd;au fyd¢ka ie,lsh yelafla

    1)   Information system / f;dr;=re moaO;shla f,i h
    2)   Automated system / iajhxl%Sh moaO;shla f,i h'
    3)   Expert system / úfYaI{ (Expert) moaO;shla f,i h'
    4)   Management support system / l<ukdlrK Wmldrl moaO;shla f,i h'
    5)   Transaction processing system / .kqfokq ieliqï moaO;shla f,i h'

                                                                                                  ( 2012 - 12 )
    Answer : 2 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    An information system is designed to collect, process, store, and distribute information, typically to
    support decision-making or data management in businesses or organizations. A washing machine does not
    manage or process information in this manner, so this classification does not apply.
    f;dr;=re moaO;shla ks¾udKh lr we;af;a f;dr;=re /ia lsÍu" ieliSu" .nvd lsÍu iy fnod yeÍu

    Option/ úl,amh 2)

    cycles with minimal user involvement beyond initial settings.

    or expert knowledge, so they are not expert systems.

 4. Consider the following models:
    my; oelafjk wdo¾Y (models) i,lkak(
    A. Network / cd,
    B. Waterfall / osh we,s
    C. Relational / iïnkaOl

    Which of the above is a/are database model/s?
    by; tajd w;=frka l=ula$l=uk tajd o;a; iuqod wdo¾Yhla$wdo¾Y fõ o?

    1) A only.         2) B only.         3) C only.         4) A and B only.     5) A and C only.
       A muKs.            B muKs.            C muKs.            A yd B muKs.         A yd C muKs.

                                                                                              ( 2012 - 21 )
    Answer : 5 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    The Network model is a type of database model where data is represented in records connected by many-
    to-many relationships using pointers. It was commonly used before the relational model became dominant.
    cd, wdlD;sh hkq o;a; iuqod wdlD;shls" tys§ o¾Yl Ndú;d lrñka nyq-isg-fndfyda iïnkaO;d u.ska
    iïnkaO lr we;s jd¾;d j, o;a; ksrEmKh flf¾' iïnkaO;d wdlD;sh m%uqL ùug fmr th nyq,j
    Ndú;d úh'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    The Waterfall model is not a database model; it is a software development life cycle (SDLC) model used
    for managing software engineering processes in a linear and sequential approach.
    Èh we,s wdlD;sh o;a; iuqod wdlD;shla fkdfõ; th f¾Çh iy wkql%ñl m%fõYhlska uDÿldx.
    bxðfkare l%shdj,Ska l<ukdlrKh lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl% SDLC
    wdlD;shls'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    The Relational model is the most widely used database model today, where data is stored in tables
    (relations) and can be queried using SQL.
    iïnkaO;d wdlD;sh wo nyq,j Ndú;d jk o;a; iuqod wdlD;sh jk w;r" tys§ o;a; j.= ^iïnkaO;d&
    ;=< .nvd lr we;s w;r SQL Ndú;fhka úuish yelsh'

    Both Network and Relational are database models

   1. Which of the following is a testing Strategy which considers the internal implementation of a program
      into account?
      l%uf,aLhl wNHka;ßl l%shdldÍ;ajh i,ld nef,k mÍlaIK Wml%uh (testing strategy) jkafka ljrlao?

          1)    Black box testing / ld, uxcqid mÍlaIKh
          2)    White box testing / Yafõ; uxcqid mÍlaIKh
          3)    Integration testing / wkql,k mÍlaIKh
          4)    Acceptance testing / ms<s.ekqu mÍlaIKh
          5)    Unit testing / tall mÍlaIKh

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2013 - 25 )
          Answer : 2 )

          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

          Option / úl,amh 1)
          Black box testing evaluates a program based on its inputs and expected outputs without any knowledge of
          the internal code or structure. Testers focus on whether the software behaves correctly from the user’s
          perspective, ignoring how the program works inside.
          ld, uxcqid mÍlaIdj uÕska jevigykla tys fhojqï iy wfmalaIs; m%;sodkhka u; mokïj wNHka;r
          fla;h fyda jHqyh ms<sn| lsisÿ oekqula fkdue;sj we.hSug ,la lrhs' mÍlaIlhska wjOdkh fhduq
          lrkafka uDÿldx.h mßYS,lhdf.a oDIaáfldaKfhka ksjerÈj l%shd lrkafkao hkakhs" jevigyk we;=<;
          l%shd lrk wdldrh fkdi,ld yßñks'

          Option / úl,amh 2)
          White box testing involves examining the internal logic, structure, and code of a program. Testers use their
          knowledge of the code to design test cases that cover all possible paths, conditions, and branches to ensure
          thorough testing of the program’s inner workings.
          Yafõ; uxcqid mÍlaIdjg jevigykl wNHka;r ;¾lkh" jHqyh iy fla;h mÍlaId lsÍu we;=<;a
          fõ' jevigyfka wNHka;r l%shdldß;ajh fyd¢ka mÍlaId lsÍu iy;sl lsÍu i|yd yels ish¨u ud¾."
          fldkafoais iy YdLd wdjrKh jk mÍlaIK wjia:d ie,iqï lsÍu i|yd mÍlaIlhska Tjqkaf.a fla;h
          ms<sn| oekqu Ndú;d lrhs'

          Option / úl,amh 3)
          Integration testing checks how different modules or components of a software system work together. It
          ensures that when individual parts are combined, they interact correctly and data flows properly between
          them.
          uDÿldx. moaO;shl úúO fudähq, fyda ixrpl tlg l%shd lrk wdldrh tAldnoaO lsÍfï mÍlaIKh
          mÍlaId lrhs' ;ks fldgia tAldnoaO jQ úg" tAjd ksjerÈj wka;¾ l%shd lrk nj;a o;a; tAjd w;r ksis
          f,i .,d hk nj;a th iy;sl lrhs'
          Option / úl,amh 4)
          Acceptance testing is performed to verify that the software meets the business requirements and is ready
          for use by the end users. It focuses on validating the system’s functionality against user needs and
          specifications, typically without regard to internal code.
          uDÿldx.h jHdmdßl wjYH;d imqrd,k nj;a wjidk mßYS,lhska úiska Ndú;hg iQodkï nj;a
          i;Hdmkh lsÍu i|yd ms<s.ekSfï mÍlaIKh isÿ lrkq ,efí' th idudkHfhka wNHka;r fla;h
          fkdi,ld mßYS,l wjYH;d iy msßú;rhkag tfrysj moaO;sfha l%shdldß;ajh j,x.= lsÍu flfrys
          wjOdkh fhduq lrhs'

    Option / úl,amh 5)
    Unit testing involves testing the smallest parts of a program, such as individual functions or methods, to
    make sure they work correctly in isolation. It usually requires knowledge of the code and is often done by
    developers during the development process.
    tAll mÍlaIKhg we;=<;a jkafka jevigykl l=vdu fldgia" ;ks ld¾hhka fyda l%u jeks tAjd yqol,dj
    ksjerÈj l%shd lrk njg j. n,d .ekSu i|yd mÍlaId lsÍuhs' tA i|yd idudkHfhka fla;h ms<sn|

 2. Which of the following information System development models has an extremely short and linear
    development process?
    my; i|yka f;dr;=re moaO;s ixj¾Ok wdlD;sj,ska b;d u flá iy f¾Çh ixj¾Ok l%shdj,shla
    we;af;a l=ulg o?
    1)   Waterfall model / Èh we<s wdlD;sh
    2)   Object-Oriented model / jia;= keUqre wdlD;sh
    3)   Spiral model / i¾ms,dldr wdlD;sh
    4)   Incremental Development model / j¾Okd;aul ixj¾Ok wdlD;sh
    5)   Rapid Application Development model / YS>% fhÿï ixj¾Ok wdlD;sh

                                                                                                     ( 2013 - 26 )
    Answer : 5 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option / úl,amh 1)
    The Waterfall model is a linear and sequential software development approach where each phase—such
    as requirements, design, implementation, testing, and maintenance—must be completed fully before
    moving to the next. This makes the process structured, predictable, and short in terms of transitions
    between phases, but it lacks flexibility for changes once a phase is completed.
    Èh we<s wdlD;sh hkq f¾Çh iy wkql%ñl uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok m%fõYhla jk w;r tys§ wjYH;d"
    ie,iqï lsÍu" l%shd;aul lsÍu" mÍlaId lsÍu iy kv;a;= lsÍu jeks iEu wÈhrlau B<Õ wÈhrg hdug
    fmr iïmQ¾Kfhkau iïmQ¾K l< hq;=h' fuh l%shdj,sh jHqy.;" mqfrdal:kh l< yels iy wÈhr
    w;r ixl%dka;s wkqj flá lrhs" kuq;a wÈhrla iïmQ¾K jQ miq fjkialï i|yd kuHYS,S njla fkdue;'
    Option / úl,amh 2)
    The Object-Oriented model focuses on designing software using objects, which are instances of classes
    combining data and behavior. It promotes modularity, reusability, and maintainability. Unlike the Waterfall
    model, it’s not inherently linear and supports iterative and evolutionary development processes, especially
    in large and complex systems.
    jia;=-keUqre wdlD;sh jia;+ka Ndú;fhka uDÿldx. ks¾udKh lsÍu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs" tAjd
    o;a; iy yeisÍï tAldnoaO lrk mka;sj, wjia:d fõ' th fudähq,ßá" kej; Ndú;d lsÍfï yelshdj
    iy kv;a;= lsÍfï yelshdj m%j¾Okh lrhs' Èh we<s wdlD;sh fuka fkdj" th wdfõKslj f¾Çh
    fkdjk w;r úfYaIfhka úYd, yd ixlS¾K moaO;sj, mqkrdj¾;k iy mßKdóh ixj¾Ok l%shdj,Ska
    i|yd iydh fõ'

    Option / úl,amh 3)
    The Spiral model is an iterative and risk-driven approach to software development. It emphasizes
    identifying and reducing risks through repeated cycles or "spirals" that include planning, risk analysis,
    engineering, and evaluation. It is more suitable for large-scale, high-risk projects and is not short or linear
    in nature.
    i¾ms,dldr wdlD;sh uDÿldx. ixj¾Okh i|yd mqkrdj¾;k iy wjodkï u; mokï jQ m%fõYhls'
    th ie,iqï lsÍu" wjodkï úYaf,aIKh" bxðfkare úoHdj iy we.hSu we;=<;a mqkrdj¾;k pl% fyda
    i¾ms,dldr yryd wjodkï y÷kd .ekSu iy wvq lsÍu wjOdrKh lrhs' th uyd mßudK" wê wjodkï
    jHdmD;s i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq jk w;r flá fyda fraÇh iajNdjhla fkdfõ'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -11-                                          #ictfromabc

    Option / úl,amh 4)
    In the Incremental model, the software is developed and delivered in small, functional parts or increments.
    Each increment adds new features and functionality, allowing user feedback and gradual improvement.
    The development process is iterative rather than linear, making it flexible and adaptable.
    j¾Okd;aul wdlD;sfha §" uDÿldx.h ixj¾Okh lr l=vd" l%shdldÍ fldgia fyda j¾Ol j,ska ,nd fokq
    ,efí' iEu j¾Olhlau kj úfYaIdx. iy l%shdldÍ;ajh tla lrhs" mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIK iy l%udkql+,
    jeäÈhqKq lsÍug bv i,ihs' ixj¾Ok l%shdj,sh fraÇh fkdj mqkrdj¾;k jk w;r th kuHYS,S iy
    wkqj¾;kh l< yels lrhs'

    Option / úl,amh 5)
    RAD is a fast-paced development model that emphasizes quick prototyping and user involvement. It
    allows for rapid changes and short development cycles, enabling teams to deliver working software in a
    short time. However, the process is not linear since it involves continuous user feedback and multiple
    iterations.
    RAD hkq blauka uQ,dlD;slrKh iy mßYS,l iyNd.S;ajh wjOdrKh lrk fõ.j;a ixj¾Ok
    wdlD;shls' th fõ.j;a fjkialï iy flá ixj¾Ok pl% i|yd bv i,ihs" lKavdhïj,g flá
    ld,hla ;=< jev lrk uDÿldx. ,nd §ug yelshdj ,nd fohs' flfia fj;;a" l%shdj,sh f¾Çh fkdfõ"
    ukao thg wLKav mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIK iy nyq mqkrdj¾;k we;=<;a fõ'

    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 5) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 3. Consider the following data flow diagram:
    my; olajd we;s o;a; .e,Sï igyk (DFD) i,lkak(

                                             B1           B2

               A                                                                  C1         C2
                                                     B3

    In the above data flow diagram A, B3 and C2 represent …… respectively.
    by; o;a; .e,Sï igyfka A, B3 iy C2 u.ska ksrEmKh jkafka ms<sfj<ska

    Which of the following terms is the most appropriate to fill the blank?

    1)   a process, an external entity and a data store / l%shdj,shla" ndysr N+;d¾:hla iy o;a; .nvdjls'
    2)   an external entity, a process and a data store / ndysr N+;d¾:hla" l%shdj,shla iy o;a; .nvdjls'
    3)   an external entity, a data store and a process / ndysr N+;d¾:hla" o;a; .nvdjla iy l%shdj,shls'
    4)   a data store, a process and an external entity / o;a; .nvdjla" l%shdj,shla iy ndysr N+;d¾:hls'
    5)   a data store, an external entity and a process / o;a; .nvdjla" ndysr N+;d¾:hla iy l%shdj,shls'

                                                                                                        ( 2013 - 28 )
    Answer : 2 )

    In a data flow diagram (DFD) / o;a; m%jdy rEm igykl ^DFD&
    External entities are represented by ovals (like "A" in your diagram).
    ndysr N+;d¾: ´j,dldr u.ska ksrEmKh flf¾ ^Tfí rEm igyfka "A" jeks&'

    Processes are represented by rectangles with subdivided sections (like the box containing B1, B2, and B3).
    l%shdj,s Wm fn§ï fldgia iys; iDcqfldaKdi% u.ska ksrEmKh flf¾ (B1, B2 iy B3 wvx.= fldgqj
    jeks&'

    Data stores are represented by open-ended rectangles (like the box containing C1 and C2).
    o;a; .nvd újD;-wjika iDcqfldaKdi% u.ska ksrEmKh flf¾ ^C1 iy C2 wvx.= fldgqj jeks&'

    Mapping the Diagram / rEm igyk is;shï.; lsÍu
    A (oval): External entity / ndysr N+;d¾:
    B3 (inside a rectangle): Process / ^iDcqfldaKdi%hla we;=<;&( l%shdj,sh

    C2 (inside an open-ended rectangle): Data store
    C2 ^újD;-wjika iDcqfldaKdi%hla we;=<;&( o;a; .nvdj

    The correct answer is / ksjerÈ ms<s;=r jkafka
    (2) an external entity, a process and a data store.
    2 ndysr N+;d¾:hla" l%shdj,shla iy o;a; .nvdjla'

    So, in the given data flow diagram, A, B3, and C2 represent an external entity, a process, and a data store,
    respectively.
    tneúka" § we;s o;a; m%jdy rEm igyfka" A & B iy C ms<sfj,ska ndysr wdh;khla" l%shdj,shla iy
    o;a; .nvdjla ksfhdackh lrhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 4. Consider the following systems in a human body:
    ñksia isrerl wvx.= my; i|yka moaO;s i,lkak(

    A. respiratory system / Yajik moaO;sh
    B. digestive system / wdydr Ô¾K moaO;sh
    C. nervous system / iakdhq moaO;sh
    D. blood circulatory system / reêr ixirK moaO;sh

    Which of the above are closed systems?
    ixjD; moaO;s jkafka l=uk tajd o?

    1) A and B only.    2) A and C only.     3) B and C only.     4) B and D only.       5) C and D only.
       A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         B yd D muKs.           C yd D muKs.

                                                                                                      ( 2013 - 29 )
    Answer : 5 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option / úl,amh A)
    The respiratory system is considered a open system. Facilitates gas exchange – taking in oxygen and
    removing carbon dioxide. it's not a closed system, Air enters the lungs from the outside environment.
    Oxygen is absorbed and carbon dioxide is exhaled back into the environment.
    Yajik moaO;sh újD; moaO;shla f,i ie,fla' jdhq yqjudrej myiq lrhs - Tlaiscka ,nd .ekSu iy
    ldnka vfhdlaihsâ bj;a lsÍu' th ixjD; moaO;shla fkdfõ" jd;h ndysr mßirfhka fmKy¨j,g
    we;=¿ fõ' Tlaiscka wjfYdaIKh lr ldnka vfhdlaihsâ kej; mßirhg uqod yßkq ,efí'

    Option / úl,amh B)
    The digestive system is an open system as it is open to the external environment at both ends — the mouth
    and the anus. Food enters the body through the mouth and waste exits through the anus. Since it directly
    interacts with the outside environment, it cannot be considered a closed system.
    wdydr Ô¾K moaO;sh újD; moaO;shls" ukao th uqLh iy .=oh hk fomiu ndysr mßirhg újD;
    fõ' wdydr uqLh yryd YÍrhg we;=¿ jk w;r wmøjH .=oh yryd msgfjhs' th ndysr mßirh
    iuÕ iDcqju wka;¾l%shd lrk neúka" th ixjD; moaO;shla f,i ie,lsh fkdyelsh'

    Option / úl,amh C)
    The nervous system is a closed system because its components, including the brain, spinal cord, and
    nerves, are all contained within protective structures like the skull and spine. Nerve signals travel internally
    and do not directly interact with the external environment, making the system functionally closed.
    iakdhq moaO;sh ixjD; moaO;shls" ukao fud<h" iqIqïkdj iy iakdhq we;=¿ tys ixrpl ish,a,u ysia
    ln, iy fld÷ weg fm< jeks wdrlaIs; jHqyhka ;=< wvx.= fõ' iakdhq ix{d wNHka;rj .uka lrk
    w;r ndysr mßirh iuÕ iDcqj wka;¾l%shd fkdlrk w;r tuÕska moaO;sh l%shdldÍj jid ouhs'

    Option / úl,amh D)
    The blood circulatory system is mostly considered a closed system because blood flows through a
    continuous network of vessels — arteries, veins, and capillaries — without leaving these pathways under
    normal conditions. However, because it interacts closely with other body systems (e.g., oxygen exchange
    in lungs or nutrient absorption in intestines), its classification can sometimes be debated.
    reêr ixirK moaO;sh fndfyda ÿrg ixjD; moaO;shla f,i ie,fla" ukao reêrh wLKav hd;ard
    cd,hla - Ouks" Ysrd iy flaYkd,sld - yryd idudkH ;;ajhka hgf;a fuu ud¾.j,ska bj;a fkdù
    .,d hhs' flfia fj;;a" th wfkl=;a YÍr moaO;s iuÕ iómj wka;¾ l%shd lrk neúka ^Wod( fmky¿
    j, Tlaiscka yqjudrej fyda nvje,aj, fmdaIl wjfYdaIKh&" tys j¾.SlrKh iuyr úg újdo l<
    yelsh'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 5) C and D only.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 5) C iy D muKs'

 6. The generation of monthly salary slips of employees in an organization is an example for
    wdh;khl fiajlhkaf.a udisl jegqma úia;r ckkh lsÍu WodyrKhla jkafka"

    1)   Batch processing / ldKav ieliqu (Batch processing) i|yd h'
    2)   Real time processing / ;;Hld, (Real time processing) ieliqu i|yd h'
    3)   Online processing / ud¾..; (Online processing) ieliqu i|yd h'
    4)   Transaction processing / .Kqfokq (Transaction processing) ieliqu i|yd h'
    5)   Interactive processing / wka;¾l%shd (Interactive processing) ieliqu i|yd h'

                                                                                                    ( 2013 - 33 )
    Answer : 1 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option / úl,amh 1)
    Batch processing is the method where data is collected over a period of time and processed all at once in
    a single run (batch). It is commonly used for tasks that do not require immediate feedback or real-time
    interaction.
    lKavdhï ieliqï hkq hï ld,hla mqrd o;a; /ia lr tljr tljr ^lKavdhula& ilik l%uhhs'
    laIKsl m%;sfmdaIK fyda ;;H ld,Sk wka;¾l%shd wjYH fkdjk ld¾hhka i|yd th nyq,j Ndú;d fõ'

    Option / úl,amh 2)
    Real-time processing handles data immediately as it is input. Salary slips don’t need immediate processing;
    they’re not urgent or time-critical per employee.
    ;;H ld,Sk ieliqï o;a; wdodkh jk úg jydu yiqrejhs' jegqma m;%sldj,g laIKsl ieliqï wjYH
    fkdfõ; tAjd fiajlfhl=g yÈis fyda ld,-;SrKd;aul fkdfõ'

    Option / úl,amh 3)
    Online processing means each transaction is processed immediately and systems are typically always
    connected (e.g., ATM transactions). Generating salary slips is not a user-initiated single transaction — it's
    a collective process done at once.
    ud¾..; ieliqï hkq iEu .kqfokqjlau jydu ilik w;r moaO;s idudkHfhka ieuúgu iïnkaO
    fõ (Wod( ATM .kqfokq)' jegqma m;%sld ckkh lsÍu hkq mßYS,lhd úiska wdrïN lrk ,o ;ks
    .kqfokqjla fkdfõ - th tljr isÿ lrk iduQysl l%shdj,shls'

    Option / úl,amh 4)
    Transaction processing handles each transaction reliably and ensures data integrity. Salary slip generation
    is not done per individual transaction or interaction — it’s done as a group.
    .kqfokq ieliSu iEu .kqfokqjlau úYajdiodhl f,i yiqrejk w;r o;a; wLKav;dj iy;sl lrhs'
    jegqma m;%sld W;amdokh ;ks .kqfokqjlg fyda wka;¾l%shdjlg isÿ fkdfõ - th lKavdhula f,i isÿ
    flf¾'

    Option / úl,amh 5)
    Interactive systems process based on user inputs, giving immediate feedback. Salary processing is
    automatic and done without ongoing interaction during the batch run.
    laIKsl m%;sfmdaIK ,nd fok mßYS,l fhojqï u; mokï jQ wka;¾l%shdldÍ moaO;s l%shdj,sh' jegqma
    ieliSu iajhxl%Sh jk w;r lKavdhï Odjkh w;r;=r wLKav wka;¾l%shd fkdue;sj isÿ flf¾'

    Therefore, option 1) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 1) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 4. Consider the following statements regarding the requirements of a Bank ATM:
    nexl= iajhxl%Sh fg,¾ hka;%hl(ATM) wjYH;d iïnkaOfhka my; fmkajd we;s j.ka;s i,ld n,kak(

    Which of the above requirements is/are functional requirement(s) of the ATM?
    by; wjYH;d w;=frka ATM ys ld¾hnoaO wjYH;dj$wjYH;d jkafk

    1) A only.         2) B only.          3) C only.       4) A and B only.      5) A and C only.
       A muKs.            B muKs.             C muKs.          A yd B muKs.          A yd C muKs..

    Answer : 4 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This is a functional requirement — it describes a specific function the ATM must provide.

    m%;sm;a;shla kshu lrhs'

    The system(s) that can be considered as open system(s) is/are
    újD; moaO;shla$moaO;s f,i i,ld ne,sh yels jkafka"

    1) A only.         2) B only.          3) C only.          4) A and B only.       5) A and C only.
       A muKs.            B muKs.             C muKs.             A yd B muKs.           A yd C muKs..

    Answer: 2)

    Option / úl,amh A)
    Not an open system in typical system theory. It is a closed loop system — blood is confined within
    vessels and doesn’t directly interact with the external environment. It's considered a closed system in
    biological and systems modeling contexts.
    idudkH moaO;s kHdhg wkqj újD; moaO;shla fkdfõ' th ixjD; Æm moaO;shls - reêrh reêr
    kd, ;=< iSud ù we;s w;r ndysr mßirh iuÕ iDcqj wka;¾ l%shd fkdlrhs' Ôj úoHd;aul iy
    moaO;s wdlD;s ks¾udK ikao¾Nhka ;=< th ixjD; moaO;shla f,i ie,fla'

    Option / úl,amh B)
    Open system. It takes in food and water (input), and excretes waste (output). This is a classic example of
    an open system — continuous exchange with the environment.
    újD; moaO;sh' th wdydr iy c,h ^wdodkh& ,nd .kakd w;r wmøjH ^m%;sodkh& neyer lrhs'
    fuh újD; moaO;shl iïNdjH WodyrKhls - mßirh iuÕ wLKav yqjudrej'

    Option / úl,amh C)
    Not a Open system. Though it exchanges signals, it doesn’t exchange physical matter or energy in the
    open-system sense, so often not classified as an open system in classical models.
    újD; moaO;shla fkdfõ' th ix{d yqjudre l<o" th újD; moaO;s w¾:fhka fN!;sl mod¾: fyda
    Yla;sh yqjudre fkdlrhs" tneúka fndfyda úg iïNdjH wdlD;sj, újD; moaO;shla f,i j¾.SlrKh
    fkdflf¾'

   1. Consider the following statement regarding an Automatic Teller Machine (ATM) of a bank:

          The time constraint ("in less than 10 seconds") specifies a performance measure → this makes it a non-
          functional requirement.
          ld, iSudj ^“;;amr 10 g wvq”& ld¾h idOk ñkqula kshu lrhs → fuh th ld¾hnoaO fkdjk
          wjYH;djhla njg m;a lrhs'

          Since this time constraint likely affects customer satisfaction, queue length, and system usability, it is
          considered essential for effective functioning and user experience.
          fuu ld, iSudj mdßfNda.sl ;Dma;sh" fmda,sï È. iy moaO;s Ndú;hg n,mdk neúka" M,odhS
          l%shdldß;ajh iy mßYS,l w;aoelSu i|yd th w;HjYH hehs ie,fla'

          Option / úl,amh 2)
          This timing is not just luxury—it affects core user satisfaction and system performance. So it's not optional.
          fuu ld, iSudj iqfLdamfNda.S fohla muKla fkdfõ - th uQ,sl mßYS,l ;Dma;shg iy moaO;s
          l%shdldß;ajhg n,mdhs' tneúka th úl,am fkdfõ'

    This statement focuses on performance timing, not the functionality itself, so it's not a functional

 3. Which of the following statements is correct with respect to openness and closeness of a system?
    moaO;shl újD;Ndjh yd ixjD;Ndjh iïnkaOfhka my; i|yka l=uk j.ka;sh ksjerÈ jkafka o?
    1) An Automatic Teller Machine of a bank should be a close system.
       nexl=jl iajhxl%Sh fg,¾ hka;%hla ixpD; moaO;shla úh hq;= h'
    2) A general purpose computer can be considered as an open system.
       fmdÿ Ndú; mß.Klhla (general purpose computer) újD; moaO;shla fia ie,lsh yels h'
    3) Human blood circulatory system is an open system.
       ñksia reêr ixirK moaO;sh újD; moaO;shls'
    4) A mobile phone is a close system
       cx.u ÿrl:kh ixjD; moaO;shls'
    5) A solar power generation system is a close system.
       iQ¾h n, ckk moaO;shla (Solar power generation system) ixjD; moaO;shls'
                                                                                                ( 2015 - 34 )
    Answer : 2 )
    To answer this question, we need to recall the basic concepts of open and closed systems:
    fuu m%Yakhg ms<s;=re §ug" újD; iy ixjD; moaO;sj, uQ,sl ixl,am wms isysm;a l< hq;=hs(
    Open System / újD; moaO;sh
    Interacts with its environment. It receives inputs and delivers outputs. Most real-world systems
    (biological, social, information) are open.
    tys mßirh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrhs' th wdodk ,nd .kakd w;r m%;sodkhka ,nd fohs' fndfyda
    ienE f,dal moaO;s ^Ôj úoHd;aul" iudÔh" f;dr;=re& újD;hs'
    Closed System / ixjD; moaO;sh
    Does not interact with the environment. It is self-contained, isolated. Almost no real system is fully
    closed - only theoretical constructs or highly controlled systems.
    mßirh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd fkdlrhs' th iajhx wka;¾.;" yqol,d fõ' lsisÿ ienE moaO;shla mdfya
    iïmQ¾Kfhkau jid ke; - kHdhd;aul bÈlsÍï fyda by< md,s; moaO;s muKs'
    Now, let's evaluate each option / oeka" wms tla tla úl,amh we.hSug ,la lruq
    Option / úl,amh 1)
    Incorrect. ATMs interact with bank systems, users, and networks. It is a highly interactive component →
    open system. / jerÈhs' iajhxl%Sh fg,¾ hka;% nexl= moaO;s" mßYS,lhska iy cd, iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd
    lrhs' th b;d wka;¾l%shdldÍ ixrplhls → újD; moaO;shls'
    Option / úl,amh 2)
    Correct. General-purpose computers interact with users, receive input, produce output, connect to
    networks—clear characteristics of an open system.
    ksjerÈ' fmdÿ ld¾h mß.Kl mßYS,lhska iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrhs" wdodk ,nd .kS" m%;sodkh
    ksmojhs" cd, fj; iïnkaO fõ - újD; moaO;shl meyeÈ,s ,laIK'
    Option / úl,amh 3)
    Incorrect. The blood circulatory system is usually considered closed, as blood stays confined within
    vessels. / jerÈhs' reêr ixirK moaO;sh idudkHfhka jid we;s nj i,lkq ,efí" ukao reêrh
    hd;%d ;=< iSud ù we;'
    Option / úl,amh 4)
    Incorrect. Mobile phones interact with networks, users, sensors, apps → they are open systems.
    jerÈhs' cx.u ÿrl:k cd," mßYS,lhska" ixfõol" fhÿï iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrhs → tAjd újD;
    moaO;s fõ'
    Option / úl,amh 5)
    Incorrect. It receives sunlight (input from environment) and provides electricity → open system.
    jerÈhs' th ysre t<sh ^mßirfhka wdodkh& ,nd .kakd w;r úÿ,sh → újD; moaO;sh imhhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.

                               2016 SYSTEM MCQ MARKING

   1. Consider the following system implementation methods:
      my; olajd we;s moaO;s l%shdjg kexùfï l%u i,ld n,kak(
           A - Direct / f¾Çh                                B – Phase / l,d                          C – Parallel / iudka;r
          Which of the above methods can be recommended to implement an emergency call handling system?
          yÈis weu;=ï fufyhqï moaO;shla l%shdjg kexùu (implement) i|yd by; i|yka l%u w;=frka
          ks¾foaY l< yelafla ljrla o?

          1) A only                     2) B only                     3) C only                     4) A and B only 5) A and C only
             A muKs                        B muKs                        C muKs                        A yd B muKs     A yd C muKs

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2016 - 21 )
          Answer : 3 )
          For an emergency call handling system, minimizing risk, and ensuring no downtime is critical because
          any interruption can result in loss of life or property. Let's review each method.
          yÈis weu;=ï yeisrùfï moaO;shla i|yd" wjodku wju lsÍu iy wl%sh ld,hla fkdue;s nj iy;sl
          lsÍu b;d jeo.;a fõ" ukao ´kEu ndOdjlska Ôú; fyda foam< wysñ úh yelsh' tla tla l%uh
          iudf,dapkh lruq'
          A. Direct Implementation / f¾Çh
          This involves switching from the old system to the new system instantly. This is not recommended for
          emergency systems because if the new system fails, there is no backup.
          fuhg merKs moaO;sfhka kj moaO;shg laIKslj udre ùu we;=<;a fõ' kj moaO;sh wiu;a jqjfyd;a
          Wmia:hla fkdue;s neúka yÈis moaO;s i|yd fuh ks¾foaY fkdlrhs'
          B. Phase Implementation / l,d
          This means introducing the new system in stages. While this can work for some business applications, in
          emergency systems, partial implementation can lead to confusion and gaps in service, as both systems
          may not be fully functional for all scenarios.
          fuhska woyia lrkafka kj moaO;sh wÈhr jYfhka y÷kajd §uhs' fuh iuyr jHdmdßl fhÿï i|yd
          l%shd l< yels jqjo" yÈis moaO;sj," w¾O jYfhka l%shd;aul lsÍu jHdl+,;ajhg iy fiajdfõ ysveiaj,g
          fya;= úh yel" ukao moaO;s folu ish¨ wjia:d i|yd iïmQ¾Kfhkau l%shdldÍ fkdúh yelsh'
          C. Parallel Implementation / iudka;r
          This involves running both the old and the new systems simultaneously for a period. This is the safest
          approach for critical systems like emergency call handling because it allows for thorough testing and
          fallback if the new system encounters issues.
          fuhg merKs iy kj moaO;s folu tljr hï ld,hla i|yd l%shd;aul lsÍu we;=<;a fõ' yÈis
          weu;=ï yeisrùu jeks ;SrKd;aul moaO;s i|yd fuh wdrlaIs;u m%fõYhhs" ukao th kj moaO;shg
          .eg¿ we;s jqjfyd;a iïmQ¾K mÍlaIK iy wdmiq yerùug bv i,ihs'
          Only parallel implementation (C) is recommended for implementing an emergency call handling system.
          Accordingly, only C is correct.
          yÈis weu;=ï yeisrùfï moaO;shla l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd iudka;r l%shd;aul lsÍu (C) muKla ks¾foaY
          flf¾' tA wkqj C muKla ksjerÈ fõ'
          Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
          tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 2. Which of the following statements is an example for an essential non-functional requirement of an
    Internet banking system?
    my; i|yka l=uk j.ka;shla wka;¾cd, nexl=lrK moaO;shl w;HjYH ld¾hnoaO fkdjk
    wjYH;djhla i|yd WodyrKhla jkafka o?

    1) System shall facilitate its users to open accounts.
       moaO;sh tys mßYS,lhkag .sKqï újD; lr .ekSug myiqlï ie,ish hq;=u h'
    2) System shall facilitate its users to check account balance.
       moaO;sh tys mßYS,lhkag Tjqkaf.a .sKqfï fYaIh mÍlaId lr ne,Sug myiqlï ie,ish hq;=u h'
    3) System shall use a 256-bit encryption for all communications.
       moaO;sh tys ish¨ ikaksfõok i|yd ìgq 256 l .=ma; fla;lhla Ndú; l< hq;=u h'
    4) System should facilitate its users to order cheque books.
       moaO;sh tys mßYS,lhkag fplafmd;a weKjqï lsÍfï myiqlï ie,ish hq;= h'
    5) System should be able to render information on all popular web browsers.
       moaO;shg ish¨ ckm%sh fjí w;ßlaiq u; f;dr;=re úoeyQ lsÍug yels úh hq;= h'
                                                                                                     ( 2016 - 32 )
    Answer : 3 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    This is a functional requirement describing a specific feature or action the system must provide to users -
    what the system does, not how it performs.
    fuh moaO;sh mßYS,lhskag ,nd Èh hq;= ksYaÑ; úfYaIdx.hla fyda l%shdjla úia;r lrk ld¾hnoaO
    wjYH;djhls - moaO;sh th l%shd lrk wdldrh fkdj" th lrkafka l=ulao hkakhs'
    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    Like (1), this is a functional requirement specifying a user operation, not a quality or performance attribute.
    1 fuka" fuh mßYS,l fufyhqula kshu lrk ld¾hnoaO wjYH;djhls" .=Kd;aulNdjh fyda ld¾h
    idOk .=Kdx.hla fkdfõ'
    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    This is an essential non-functional requirement because it specifies a critical quality attribute of the system
    - security. Non-functional requirements define how a system performs its functions, focusing on aspects
    like security, performance, reliability, and usability. Using 256-bit encryption ensures that all data
    transmitted between users and the system is protected against unauthorized access and breaches, which is
    vital for Internet banking systems handling sensitive financial information.
    fuh w;HjYH ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;djhls" ukao th moaO;sfha ;SrKd;aul .=Kd;aul .=Kdx.hla
    - wdrlaIdj kshu lrhs' ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d uÕska moaO;shla tys ld¾hhka bgq lrk wdldrh
    ks¾jpkh lrhs" wdrlaIdj" ld¾h idOkh" úYajikSh;ajh iy Ndú;h jeks wxY flfrys wjOdkh
    fhduq lrhs' 256-bit ixfla;kh Ndú;d lsÍu uÕska mßYS,lhska iy moaO;sh w;r iïfm%aIKh jk
    ish¨u o;a; wkjir m%fõYfhka iy lvlsÍï j,ska wdrlaId jk nj iy;sl lrhs" th ixfõ§ uQ,H
    f;dr;=re yiqrejk wka;¾cd, nexl= moaO;s i|yd w;HjYH fõ'
    Option/ úl,amh 4)

    or data before they are processed or filed permanently. A file tray is typically used to hold papers
    temporarily on a desk or workspace, acting as a transient storage area before further action is taken. It is
    easily accessible and intended for short-term manual handling.
    w;ahqre ;djld,sl o;a; .nvdjla hkq f,aLk fyda o;a; ieliSug fyda iaÓrj f.dkq lsÍug fmr

    w;r flá ld,Sk w;ska yeisrùu i|yd woyia flf¾'

    It is more permanent and less convenient for quick, temporary use.

                                        B

    Answer : 5 )

    To determine the correct high-level data flow diagram with respect to the rules on data flow modeling, we
    need to consider standard conventions. In data flow diagrams (DFDs), the following rules typically apply.
    o;a; m%jdy wdlD;slrKh ms<sn| kS;sÍ;sj,g wod<j ksjerÈ by< uÜgfï o;a; m%jdy rEm igyk
    ;SrKh lsÍu i|yd" wms iïu; iïuq;Ska i,ld ne,sh hq;=h' o;a; m%jdy rEm igyka (DFDs) j,§"
    my; i|yka kS;s idudkHfhka wod< fõ'

    •    Processes (e.g., B) are represented by rectangles and transform incoming data flows into outgoing data
         flows.
         l%shdj,s (Wod( B) iDcqfldaKdi% u.ska ksrEmKh jk w;r tk o;a; m%jdy msg;g hk o;a; m%jdy
         njg mßj¾;kh fõ'

    •    Data stores (e.g., the box with "1" inside) hold data and are connected to processes with two-way
         arrows if data is both read from and written to the store.
         o;a; .nvd (Wod: "1" we;=<; we;s fldgqj) o;a; r|jd ;nd .kakd w;r o;a; .nvdfjka lshjd
         ,shd we;akï oaú-ud¾. B;, iys; l%shdj,Skag iïnkaO fõ'

    •    External entities (e.g., A and C) are represented by ovals and interact with the system by sending or
         receiving data.
         ndysr wdh;k (Wod( A iy C) ´j,dldr u.ska ksrEmKh jk w;r o;a; heùfuka fyda ,eîfuka
         moaO;sh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrhs'

    •    Data flows are represented by arrows, showing the direction of data movement.
         o;a; m%jdy B;, u.ska ksrEmKh jk w;r o;a; p,kfha ÈYdj fmkajhs'

    •    A process should have at least one input and one output data flow.
         l%shdj,shlg wju jYfhka tla wdodkhla iy tla m%;sodk o;a; m%jdyhla ;sìh hq;=h'

    Analyzing the diagrams / rEm igyka úYaf,aIKh lsÍu

    Option/ úl,amh 1)

    Answer : 4 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Below are some of the key DFD rules
    my; oelafjkafka m%Odk DFD kS;s lsysmhls

    •   Processes (rectangles with rounded corners) must have at least one input and one output.
        l%shdj,s ^jgl=re fldka iys; iDcqfldaKdi%& j,g wju jYfhka tla wdodkhla iy m%;sodkhla ;sìh
        hq;=h'

    •   Data stores (open-ended rectangles) must be connected to a process with at least one incoming and
        one outgoing data flow. / o;a; .nvd ^újD;-wjika iDcqfldaKdi%& wju jYfhka tla tk iy tla
        msg;g hk o;a; .e,Sula iys; l%shdj,shlg iïnkaO l< hq;=h'

    •   External entities (ovals) can send data to or receive data from a process, but not directly from another
        external entity or data store. / ndysr N=;d¾: ^´j,dldr& l%shdj,shlg o;a; heùug fyda ,nd .ekSug
        yels kuq;a fjk;a ndysr N=;d¾:hlska fyda o;a; .nvdjlska iDcqju fkdfõ'

    •   Data must flow through a process; it cannot move directly from one data store to another or from a
        data store to an external entity without passing through a process.
        o;a; l%shdj,shla yryd .,d hd hq;=h; th l%shdj,shla yryd .uka fkdlr tla o;a; .nvdjlska
        ;j;a .nvdjlg fyda o;a; .nvdjlska ndysr N=;d¾:hlg iDcqju .uka l< fkdyel'

    Explanation of each option / tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option / úl,am 1)
    Here, only shows input from external entity A to Process B. It is invalid. Every process must have at least
    one output. / fuys" ndysr N=;d¾:h A isg l%shdj,sh B olajd wdodkh muKla fmkajhs' th j,x.=
    fkdfõ' iEu l%shdj,shlgu wju jYfhka tla m%;sodkhla ;sìh hq;=h'

    Option / úl,am 2)
    Here, only shows output from Process B to external entity A. It is invalid: Every process must have at least
    one input. / fuys" B l%shdj,sfha isg ndysr N=;d¾:h A olajd m%;sodkh muKla fmkajhs' th j,x.=
    fkdfõ( iEu l%shdj,shlgu wju jYfhka tla wdodkhlaj;a ;sìh hq;=h'

    Option / úl,am 3)
    Here, process B has both input and output from/to the same external entity A. It is not a best practice.
    Although possible, it doesn’t clearly show interaction with a data store or another entity.
    fuys" B l%shdj,shg tlu ndysr N=;d¾:h A fj;ska$fj; wdodkh iy m%;sodkh hk folu we;' th
    fyd|u ms<sfj;la fkdfõ' yels jqjo" th o;a; .nvdjla fyda fjk;a N=;d¾:hla iuÕ wka;¾l%shd
    meyeÈ,sj fkdfmkajhs'

    Option / úl,am 4)
    Here, process B receives input from A and sends output to a data store M1, which then leads to external
    entity C. It is correct. Respects all DFD rules, the process has an input and an output, and data flows
    through a data store before reaching another entity.
    fuys" B l%shdj,sh A fj;ska wdodkh ,nd .kakd w;r o;a; .nvdjlg m%;sodkh M1 fj; hjhs" th
    miqj ndysr N=;d¾:h C fj; fhduq lrhs' th ksjerÈhs' ish¨u DFD kS;sj,g .re lrhs" l%shdj,shg
    wdodkhla iy m%;sodkhla we;s w;r" o;a; fjk;a N=;d¾:hlg <Õd ùug fmr o;a; .nvdjla yryd
    .,d hhs'

    Option / úl,am 5)
    Here, external entity A receives data from data store M1 directly. It is invalid. Data stores should only
    exchange data with processes, not directly with external entities.
    fuys" ndysr N=;d¾:h A o;a; .nvdfjka M1 iDcqju o;a; ,nd .kS' th j,x.= fkdfõ' o;a; .nvd
    l< hq;af;a l%shdj,s iuÕ muKla jk w;r ndysr N=;d¾: iuÕ iDcqju fkdfõ'

 4. Requirements of a system can be classified as Mandatory (Essential), Desirable (Optional),
    Functional and Non-functional.
    w;HdjYH" w;HdjYH fkdjk" ld¾hnoaO iy ld¾hnoaO fkdjk f,i moaO;shl wjYH;d j¾.SlrKh
    l< yels h'

    “ATM of a bank shall not allow more than three attempts to enter a user’s secret number incorrectly”
    Which of the followings correctly classifies the above requirement?
    "nexl=jl ATM hka;%hla mßYS,lhl=g jerÈ f,i ryia wxlh we;=<;a lsÍug W;aidyl wjia:d
    ;=klg jvd bv ,nd fkdÈh hq;=u h'" by; i|yka wjYH;dj ksjerÈj j¾.SlrKh lrkafka my;
    i|yka l=ulska o?

    1)   Mandatory, Functional / w;HdjYH" ld¾hnoaO
    2)   Mandatory, Non-functional / w;HdjYH" ld¾hnoaO fkdjk
    3)   Desirable, Functional / w;HdjYH fkdjk" ld¾hnoaO
    4)   Desirable, Non-functional / w;HdjYH fkdjk" ld¾hnoaO fkdjk
    5)   Mandatory or Desirable, Functional / w;HdjYH fyda w;HdjYH fkdjk" ld¾hnoaO

                                                                                                    ( 2017 - 30 )
    Answer : 2 )
    We need to classify this in two ways.
    wms fuh wdldr follska j¾.SlrKh l< hq;=hs'

    Mandatory vs. Desirable
    Mandatory means the system must have it. It's essential.
    wksjd¾hh hkafkka woyia lrkafka moaO;shg th ;sìh hq;=h' th w;HjYHhs'

    Desirable means it would be nice to have, but the system can still work without it.
    wksjd¾h hkafkka woyia lrkafka th ;sîu fyd|hs" kuq;a moaO;shg th fkdue;sj ;ju;a l%shd l<
    yelsh'

    Is it mandatory or desirable? If an ATM let you try a PIN infinite times, anyone could guess someone's
    PIN eventually. This is a huge security risk. So, limiting attempts is absolutely mandatory for the ATM to
    be safe and usable for banking.
    th wksjd¾ho ke;fyd;a fhda.Ho@ ATM tlla Tng wkka; jdrhla PIN tlla W;aidy lsÍug bv
    fokafka kï" ´kEu flfkl=g wjidkfha flfkl=f.a PIN wxlh wkqudk l< yelsh' fuh úYd,
    wdrlaIl wjodkuls' tneúka" ATM wdrlaIs; iy nexl= lghq;= i|yd Ndú;d l< yels ùug W;aidyhka
    iSud lsÍu w;HjYH fõ'

    Functional vs. Non-functional
    Functional describes what the system does (e.g., "The ATM lets you withdraw cash"). It's a specific action
    or capability. / ld¾hnoaO moaO;sh lrkafka l=ulao hkak úia;r lrhs ^Wod( ATM Tng uqo,a wdmiq
    .ekSug bv fohs&' th ksYaÑ; l%shdjla fyda yelshdjls'

    Non-functional describes how the system works, or its qualities (e.g., how fast it is, how secure it is, how
    reliable it is). / ld¾hnoaO fkdjk hkq moaO;sh l%shd lrk wdldrh fyda tys .=Kdx. ^Wod( th fl;rï
    fõ.j;ao" th fl;rï wdrlaIs;o" th fl;rï úYajdiodhlo hkak& úia;r lrhs'

    Is it functional or non-functional?
    The ATM's function is to let you enter your PIN and verify it. The rule "not allow more than three attempts"
    isn't a new function itself. Instead, it's a security rule that limits how the PIN entry function can be used.
    It's about the quality of the system's security. Therefore, it's non-functional.
    ATM ys ld¾hh jkafka Tfí PIN wxlh we;=<;a lr th i;Hdmkh lsÍug Tng bv §uhs' W;aidy
    ;=klg jvd bv fkdfokak hk Í;sh kj ld¾hhla fkdfõ' tA fjkqjg" th PIN we;=<;a lsÍfï Y%s;h
    Ndú;d l< yels wdldrh iSud lrk wdrlaIl Í;shls' th moaO;sfha wdrlaIdfõ .=Kd;aulNdjh .ek
    h' tneúka" th ld¾hnoaO fkdfõ'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -36-                                        #ictfromabc

    Putting it together:
    The requirement is Mandatory because it's essential for security, and Non-functional because it describes
    a quality (security) rather than a direct action of the system.
    wjYH;djh wdrlaIdj i|yd w;HjYH neúka wksjd¾h jk w;r" moaO;sfha iDcq l%shdjlg jvd
    .=Kd;aul Ndjhla ^wdrlaIdjla& úia;r lrk neúka ld¾hnoaO fkdfõ'

    So, the correct classification is Mandatory, Non-functional.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ j¾.SlrKh wksjd¾h" ld¾hnoaO fkdfõ'

    Option / úl,amh 1)
    While it's mandatory, classifying it purely as functional might overlook its primary role as a security
    constraint. / th wksjd¾h jqjo" th iïmQ¾Kfhkau ld¾hnoaO f,i j¾.SlrKh lsÍfuka wdrlaIl
    ndOlhla f,i tys m%Odk ld¾hNdrh fkdi,ld yeßh yel'

    Option / úl,amh 3)
    Incorrect because this is not an optional feature; it’s critical for security.
    fuh úl,am úfYaIdx.hla fkdjk ksid jerÈhs; th wdrlaIdj i|yd b;d jeo.;a fõ'

    Option / úl,amh 4)
    Incorrect for both classification types.
    j¾.SlrK j¾. folgu jerÈhs'

    Option / úl,amh 5)
    Incorrect as it's definitely mandatory and more precisely non-functional.
    th ksielju wksjd¾h jk w;r jvd;a ksjerÈj ld¾hnoaO fkdjk neúka jerÈhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 5. Which of the following organs is a part of a closed system of the human body?
    my; i|yka l=uk bkaøsh ñksia YÍrfha ixjD; moaO;shl fldgila fõ o?

    1) Eye              2) Ear              3) Heart          4) Kidney          5) Lung
       wei                 lk                  yDoh              jl=.vqj            fmkye,a,

                                                                                                  ( 2017 - 37 )
    Answer: 3)

    Open System / újD; moaO;sh
    A system that interacts with the external environment by exchanging matter or energy.
    mod¾:h fyda Yla;sh yqjudre lr .ksñka ndysr mßirh iuÕ wka;¾l%shd lrk moaO;shls'

    Closed System / ixjD; moaO;sh
    A system that does not exchange matter with the environment but may exchange energy.
    mßirh iuÕ mod¾: yqjudre fkdlrk kuq;a Yla;sh yqjudre lr .; yels moaO;shls'

    Option / úl,am 1)
    Part of the sensory system, not a closed-loop system.
    ixjD; Æm moaO;shla fkdj" ixfõol moaO;sfha fldgils'

    Option / úl,am 2)
    Also part of the sensory system, specifically hearing and balance, not closed.
    ixfõol moaO;sfha fldgila" úfYaIfhka Y%jKh iy iunr;djh" ixjD; fkdfõ'

    Option / úl,am 3)
    The heart is part of the circulatory system, which is a closed system in the human body. In a closed
    circulatory system, blood is contained within vessels (arteries, veins, and capillaries) and is pumped by
    the heart throughout the body without leaving these vessels.
    yoj; hkq ñksia isrefra ixjD; moaO;shla jk ixirK moaO;sfha fldgils' ixjD; ixirK moaO;shl"
    reêrh reêr kd, ^Ouks" Ysrd iy flaYkd,sld& ;=< wvx.= jk w;r fuu kd, j,ska bj;a fkdù
    yoj; YÍrh mqrd fmdïm lrkq ,efí'

    Option / úl,am 4)
    Part of the excretory system, which is an open system as it expels waste.
    wmøjH neyer lrk újD; moaO;shla jk neyer lsÍfï moaO;sfha fldgils'

    Option / úl,am 5)
    Part of the respiratory system, which interacts with the external environment (open system)
    ndysr mßirh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrk Yajik moaO;sfha fldgila ^újD; moaO;sh&

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 6. Consider the following system implementation methods:
    my; i|yka moaO;s l%shd;aul lsÍfï l%u i,lkak(

    A - Direct / f¾Çh
    B - Pilot / kshdul
    C - Parallel / iudka;r

    Which of the above method(s) is/are generally used to implement a home security system?
    by; i|yka l=uk l%uh$l%u idudkHfhka .Dyia: wdrlaIl moaO;shla l%shd;aul lsÍug Ndú; lrkq
    ,nkafka o?

    1) A only                          2) B only                          3) C only
       A muKs                             B muKs                             C muKs
    4) A and B only                    5) A and C only
       A iy B muKs                        A iy C muKs

                                                                                                   ( 2017 - 49 )

    Answer: 5)

    System Implementation Methods / moaO;s l%shd;aul lsÍfï l%u

    A - Direct: The old system is immediately replaced with the new one. Suitable for small, low-risk systems.
    iDcq( merKs moaO;sh jydu kj tl iuÕ m%;sia:dmkh fõ' l=vd" wvq wjodkï moaO;s i|yd iqÿiq fõ'

    B - Pilot: The new system is introduced in a limited scope first, then fully deployed if successful. Usually
    used in large organizations.
    kshuq( kj moaO;sh m<uqj iSñ; úIh m:hlska y÷kajd fokq ,efí" miqj id¾:l kï iïmQ¾Kfhkau
    fhdojkq ,efí' idudkHfhka úYd, ixúOdkj, Ndú;d fõ'

    C - Parallel: The old and new systems run simultaneously for a period. Used when reliability is critical.
    iudka;r( merKs iy kj moaO;s hï ld,hla i|yd tljr l%shd;aul fõ' úYajikSh;ajh b;d jeo.;a
    jk úg Ndú;d fõ'

    Now apply this to a home security system
    oeka fuh ksjdi wdrlaIl moaO;shlg fhdouq

    Direct / iDcq
    This is common for home systems — install and switch over to the new system immediately.
    fuh ksjdi moaO;s i|yd fmdÿ fõ - jydu kj moaO;shg ia:dmkh lr udre jkak'

    Parallel / iudka;r
    In some cases, especially where security is critical, both old and new systems might be used in parallel
    temporarily to ensure the new one works reliably (e.g., testing a new system while keeping the old
    alarms active).
    iuyr wjia:dj,§" úfYaIfhka wdrlaIdj b;d jeo.;a jk úg" kj tl úYajdiodhl f,i l%shd
    lrk nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd merKs iy kj moaO;s folu ;djld,slj iudka;rj Ndú;d l<
    yelsh ^Wod( merKs wk;=re weÕùï l%shdldÍj ;nd .ksñka kj moaO;shla mÍlaId lsÍu&'

    Pilot / kshuq
    Less applicable in a home environment because it doesn’t make much sense to “pilot” a system in one
    room — homes are small-scale, unlike large organizations.
    tla ldurhl moaO;shla kshuq lsÍu t;rï f;areula ke;s ksid .Dy mßirhl wvq wod<;ajhla we;
    - ksjdi úYd, ixúOdk fuka fkdj l=vd mßudKfhka hqla; fõ'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 5).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 5) fõ'

                               2018 SYSTEM MCQ MARKING

   1. Consider the following statements relevant to external entities of Data Flow Diagrams.
      o;a; .e,Sï igykaj, ndysr N+;d¾:j,g (external entities) wod< my; m%ldY i,lkak'
          A. An external entity can be a person, system or organization that has predefined behaviour.
             ndysr N+;d¾:" mQ¾j w¾:olajk ,o yeisÍï rgdjla we;s mqoa.,hl=" moaO;shla fyda ixúOdkhla
             úh yel'
          B. An external entity can be a source of input data for a process or/and a destination of the output
             of a process. / ndysr N=;d¾:" l%shdj,shla (process) i|yd wdodk o;a; m%Njhla fyda$iy l%shdj,shl
             m%;sodk wka;hla úh yel'
          C. External entities are always data stores.
             ndysr N+;d¾: ieu úgu o;a; .nvdjka fõ'
          Which of the above statement(s) is/are correct?
          by; ljr j.ka;shla i;H fõ o?

          1) A only.                    2) B only.                    3) A and B only.              4) B and C only.                  5) All A, B and C.
             A muKs.                       B muKs.                       A yd B muKs.                  B yd C muKs.                      A, B iy C ish,a,u'

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2018 - 27 )
          Answer : 3 )

          Explanation of each option
          tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu

          Statement / m%ldYh A)
          This option is correct. In Data Flow Diagrams, external entities are elements that exist outside the system's
          boundaries. These can be people, other systems, or even organizations that interact with the system.
          fuu úl,amh ksjerÈhs' o;a; .e,Sï igyka j,§" ndysr N+;d¾: hkq moaO;sfha iSudjkaf.ka msg;
          mj;sk uQ,dx. fõ' fïjd moaO;sh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrk mqoa.,hska" fjk;a moaO;s fyda ixúOdk mjd
          úh yelsh'

          They typically have predefined behavior, meaning their interactions with the system are predictable and
          well-defined. External entities are sources or destinations of data, but they are not part of the system's
          internal processes.
          tAjdg idudkHfhka mQ¾j ksYaÑ; yeisÍï we;" tkï moaO;sh iuÕ Tjqkaf.a wka;¾l%shd mqfrdal:kh
          l< yels iy fyd¢ka w¾: olajd we;' ndysr N+;d¾: hkq o;a;j, uQ,dY% fyda .ukdka; fõ" kuq;a
          tAjd moaO;sfha wNHka;r l%shdj,Skays fldgila fkdfõ'

          Statement / m%ldYh B)
          This option is also correct. External entities either provide input data to the system or receive output data
          from the system. The flow of data between external entities and the system is represented by data flow
          arrows in a DFD.
          fuu úl,amh o ksjerÈ h' ndysr N+;d¾: moaO;shg wdodk o;a; imhhs fyda moaO;sfhka m%;sodk o;a;
          ,nd .kS' ndysr N+;d¾: iy moaO;sh w;r o;a; m%jdyh" o;a; m%jdy B;, u.ska ksrEmKh flf¾'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -40-                                          #ictfromabc

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This option is incorrect. External entities are not the same as data stores in a DFD. Data stores are used to
    represent storage of data within the system, where data is kept for future use. On the other hand, external
    entities represent sources or destinations of data that interact with the system, but they are not where the
    data is stored within system. Data stores exist within system and external entities exist outside the system.
    fuu úl,amh jerÈhs' ndysr N+;d¾: iy o;a; .nvd iudk fkdfõ' o;a; .nvd moaO;sh ;=< o;a;
    .nvd lsÍu ksrEmKh lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs" tys§ o;a; wkd.; Ndú;h i|yd ;nd we;' wfkla w;g
    ndysr N+;d¾:" moaO;sh iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrk o;a;j, m%Njhka fyda .ukdka; ksfhdackh lrhs"
    kuq;a tAjd moaO;sh ;=< o;a; .nvd lr we;s ia:dk fkdfõ' o;a; .nvd, moaO;sh ;=< mj;sk w;r
    ndysr N+;d¾: moaO;sfhka msg; mj;S'

    Therefore, the answer is 3) A and B only
    tneúka ms<s;=r 3) A iy B mu”

 2. Consider the following tasks.
    my; oelafjk ld¾h i,ld n,kak'
    A. identifying the problems in the existing system
       oekg mj;sk moaO;sfha .eg¿ y÷kd .ekSu
    B. suggesting alternative solutions
       úl,am úi÷ï fhdackd lsÍu
    C. prioritizing of the information system's requirements
       f;dr;=re moaO;sfha wjYH;d m%uqL;ajhg wkqj fm< .eiaùu
    Which of the above tasks is/are carried out during the preliminary investigations of systems
    development life cycle? / by; ld¾hj,ska ljrla moaO;s ixj¾Ok cSj pl%fhys uQ,sl úu¾Ykfha§
    (preliminary investigations) bgq lrkq ,efí o?

    1) A only.           2) A and B only.      3) A and C only.    4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
       A muKs.              A yd B muKs.          A yd C muKs.        B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                    ( 2018 - 29 )
    Answer : 5 )
    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This step involves analyzing the current system to detect issues such as inefficiencies, errors, or outdated
    processes. / fuu mshjrg wld¾hlaIu;d" fodaI fyda h,a mek .sh l%shdj,Ska jeks .eg¿ y÷kd .ekSu
    i|yd j;auka moaO;sh úYaf,aIKh lsÍu we;=<;a fõ'

    It helps determine whether a new system is necessary or if the current one needs modifications.
    th kj moaO;shla wjYHo ke;fyd;a j;auka moaO;shg fjkia lsÍï wjYHo hkak ;SrKh lsÍug
    WmldÍ fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    Once the problems are identified, the next step is to explore different ways to solve them.
    .eg¿ y÷kd.;a miq" B<Õ mshjr jkafka tAjd úi£ug úúO l%u .fõIKh lsÍuhs'

    These alternatives could include upgrading the current system, developing a new one, or even making
    process changes without new technology.
    fuu úl,am w;rg j;auka moaO;sh W;afY%aKs lsÍu" kj tlla ixj¾Okh lsÍu fyda kj ;dlaIKh
    fkdue;sj l%shdj,s fjkialï isÿ lsÍu mjd we;=<;a úh yelsh'

 3. Which of the following feasibility types is generally not carried out during the development of a

    my; ljr YlH;d m%rEmh wOHhkh lsÍu idudkHfhka isÿ fkdfõ o?

    4)   organizational feasibility / ixúOdkuh YlH;dj

    Answer : 2 )

    A feasibility study is a comprehensive analysis conducted to assess the practicality and viability of a
    proposed project or system.
    YlH;d wOHhkhla hkq fhdað; jHdmD;shl fyda moaO;shl m%dfhda.sl;ajh iy YlH;dj ;lafiare lsÍu
    i|yd mj;ajkq ,nk mq¿,a úYaf,aIKhls'

    A feasibility study assesses whether a project is practical. In an in-house system, the following types are
    relevant: / YlH;d wOHhkhla uÕska jHdmD;shla m%dfhda.slo hkak ;lafiare lrhs' wNHka;r moaO;shl"
    my; j¾. wod< fõ(

    th jHdmdßl wjYH;d imqrd,k nj iy;sl lsÍu'

    Organizational feasibility / wdh;ksl YlH;dj

    th iud.fï jHqyh ;=<g .e,fmk njg j. n,d .ekSu'

    Technical feasibility / ;dlaIKSl YlH;dj
    Evaluating if the company has the required technology.

    However, Market Feasibility (option 2) is about analyzing demand and competition, which is irrelevant
    for an in-house system because the system is used internally, not sold in the market.

 4. Consider the following statements regarding functional and non-functional requirements of a sales
    system./wf,ú moaO;shl (sales system) ld¾hnoaO(functional) iy ld¾hnoaO fkdjk(non-functional)
    wjYH;d iïnkaO my; m%ldY i,lkak'

    I    - Reads barcodes of items purchased and produces the invoice

    II - Accepts user’s request and responds in less than 1 second

    2)   I - functional, II and III - non-functional / I – ld¾hnoaO, II iy III – ld¾hnoaO fkdjk

    4)   I and III - functional, II - non-functional / I iy III – ld¾hnoaO, II – ld¾hnoaO fkdjk

    Answer : 2 )

    Functional Requirements describe what the system does (specific operations, tasks, or behaviors).
    ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d uÕska moaO;sh lrkafka l=ulao ^ksYaÑ; fufyhqï" ld¾hhka fyda yeisÍï& úia;r
    flf¾'

    ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d uÕska moaO;sh fl;rï fyd¢ka l%shd lrhso ^ld¾h idOkh" wdrlaIdj"

    This is a core feature of a sales system (the actual function).

 6. Consider the following statements regarding Structured and Object Oriented software development
    methodologies. / jHqy.; (structured) iy jia;= keUqre (object oriented) uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok l%ufõo
    ms<sn| my; oelafjk m%ldY i,lkak'
    A. Structured analysis and design represents systems as a hierarchy of functions.
       jHqy.; úYaf,aIKh yd ks¾udKh moaO;s Y%s; OQrdj,shla (function hierarchy) f,i olajhs'
    B. Structured design is a system of interacting objects.
       jHqy.; ks¾udKh (structured design) hkq wka;¾ l%shdldÍ jia;= wvx.= moaO;shls'
    C. Object oriented methodology combines data and processes into individual entities.
       jia;= keUqre l%ufõoh o;a; iy l%shdhk (processes) ;ks N+;d¾:j,g (entities) ixhqla; lrhs'
    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; m%ldY w;=frka ljrla i;H fõ o?

    1) A only.         2) B only.          3) A and B only.    4) A and C only.      5) B and C only.
       A muKs.            B muKs.             A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.          B yd C muKs'

                                                                                                  ( 2018 - 45 )
    Answer: 3)

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This statement is correct. CoStructured analysis and design focus on breaking down a system into a
    hierarchy of functions using a top-down approach. This approach emphasizes functions rather than objects.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' jHqy.; úYaf,aIKh iy ks¾udKh by<-my< m%fõYhla Ndú;d lrñka
    moaO;shla Y%s;j, OQrdj,shlg ì| oeóu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs' fuu m%fõYh jia;+kag jvd
    Y%s; wjOdrKh lrhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This statement is incorrect.Structured design does not focus on objects. Instead, it is based on procedures
    and functions. Objects are a key feature of Object-Oriented Programming (OOP), not structured design.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈhs' jHqy.; ks¾udKh jia;+ka flfrys wjOdkh fhduq fkdlrhs' tA fjkqjg" th l%shd
    mámdá iy Y%s; u; mokï fõ' jia;= hkq jHqy.; ks¾udKh fkdj jia;=-keUqre l%uf,aLkfha ^OOP&
    m%Odk ,laIKhls'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This statement is correct. Object-Oriented methodology combines data and processes (methods) into
    objects. This is the core principle of Object-Oriented Programming, which includes concepts like
    encapsulation,inheritance, and polymorphism.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' jia;=-keUqre l%ufõoh o;a; iy l%shdj,Ska ^l%u& jia;+ka njg tAldnoaO lrhs'
    fuh jia;=-keUqre l%uf,aLkfha uQ,sl uQ,O¾uh jk w;r thg lemaishqf,aIka" Wreuh iy nyqrEm;dj
    jeks ixl,am we;=<;a fõ'

    Therefore the answer is 3) A and C only
    tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3) A iy C mu”

          uDÿldx. moaO;sh mÍlaId lsÍu'

          The question "Which of the following shows the correct order of software testing?" is asking you to
          identify the sequence in which different types of software testing are typically conducted during the
          software development lifecycle.

    Software testing typically starts with Unit Testing to check individual components. Next, Integration
    Testing verifies the interactions between combined units. Then, System Testing evaluates the complete
    system's compliance with requirements. Finally, Acceptance Testing ensures the software meets business
    needs and is ready for deployment.
    uDÿldx. mÍlaIdj idudkHfhka wdrïN jkafka ;ks ixrpl mÍlaId lsÍu i|yd tAll mÍlaIdfjks'
    B<Õg" tAldnoaO mÍlaIdj tAldnoaO tAll w;r wka;¾l%shd ;yjqre lrhs' bkamiqj" moaO;s mÍlaIdj
    uÕska iïmQ¾K moaO;sfha wjYH;d iuÕ wkql+, ùu we.hSug ,la lrhs' wjidk jYfhka" m%;s.%yK
    mÍlaIdj uÕska uDÿldx.h jHdmdßl wjYH;d imqrd,k w;r fhoùug iQodkï nj iy;sl lrhs'

 2. A software development company identifies that their new system development project has complex
    requirements and has a medium to high risk level. Further, an evaluation is needed to clear the
    requirements and significant changes are expected during system development.
    uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok iud.ula ;u kj moaO;s ixj¾Ok jHdmD;sh ixlS¾K wjYH;dj,ska iukaú; nj;a"
    uOHu (medium) isg by< (high) olajd wjodkï (risk) uÜgula we;s nj;a y÷kd.kshs' ;jo wjYH;d

    What is the most suitable software development process model for this project?
    fuu jHdmD;sh i|yd jvd;au iqÿiq uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok l%shdj,s wdlD;sh (software development process
    model) l=ula o?
    1)   agile / iqp,H
    2)   prototyping / uQ,dlD;slrKh

    4)   spiral /i¾ms,

                                                                                                 ( 2019 – 07 )
    Answer: 4)

    Agile / iqp,H
    A flexible and iterative approach where development is done in small increments called sprints, focusing
    on collaboration and adaptability.
    iyfhda.S;djh iy wkqj¾;kh ùu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrñka" iam%skaÜ f,i y÷kajk l=vd j¾Ol
    j,ska ixj¾Okh isÿ flfrk kuHYS,S iy mqkrdj¾;k m%fõYhls'

    Prototyping / uQ,dlD;slrKh
    Involves creating a preliminary version (prototype) of the software to better understand its functionality
    and requirements through iterative feedback.
    mqkrdj¾;k m%;sfmdaIK yryd tys l%shdldÍ;ajh iy wjYH;d jvd fyd¢ka wjfndaO lr .ekSu i|yd
    uDÿldx.fha uQ,sl wkqjdohla ^uQ, wdlD;shla& ks¾udKh lsÍu we;=<;a fõ'

    Rapid Application Development (RAD) / YS> fhÿï ixj¾Okh
    Emphasizes quick development and iteration of prototypes for rapid adjustments based on user feedback,
    aiming for fast delivery.
    fõ.j;a fnodyeÍu b,lal lr .ksñka mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIK u; mokïj fõ.j;a .e,mSï i|yd
    uQ,dlD;sj, blauka ixj¾Okh iy mqkrdj¾;kh wjOdrKh lrhs'

    Spiral / i¾ms,dldr
    Combines iterative development with risk assessment, designed for projects with high uncertainty and
    complex requirements, allowing continuous evaluation and refinement.
    wjodkï ;lafiarej iuÕ mqkrdj¾;k ixj¾Okh tAldnoaO lrhs" by< wúksYaÑ;;djhlska iy ixlS¾K
    wjYH;d iys; jHdmD;s i|yd ks¾udKh lr we;" wLKav we.hSug iy fYdaOkhg bv i,ihs'

    Waterfall / Èh we,s
    A linear and sequential approach where each phase of development must be completed before moving on
    to the next, suitable for projects with well-defined requirements.
    fyd¢ka w¾: olajd we;s wjYH;d iys; jHdmD;s i|yd iqÿiq" B<Õg hdug fmr ixj¾Okfha iEu
    wÈhrlau iïmQ¾K l< hq;= f¾Çh iy wkql%ñl m%fõYhls'

    The model includes prototyping and iterative evaluation, ensuring that requirements are thoroughly
