   1. Which of the following statement/s regarding the testing of a system are correct?
      moaO;shl yß jerÈ ne,Su (mÍlaId lsÍu / testing) iïnkaOfhka my; ljr j.ka;s$h ksjerÈ fõ o?
          A. Black-box testing involves detailed checking of each line in the code.
             ld, uxcqid mÍlaIdjg(black box testing) fla;fhys iEu fma<shlau úis;rd;aulj mÍlaIdj wvx.=fõ'
          B. Unit-testing helps to uncover errors in the codes.
             tall mÍlaIdj (unit testing) fla;j, fodaI wkdjrKh lsÍug WmldÍ fõ'
          C. System testing should not be performed prior to unit-testing.
             tall mÍlaIdjg fmr moaO;s mßlaIdj isÿ fkdl< hq;= h'

          1) A only.                     2) B only.                      3) C only.                     4) A and C only.                5) B and C only.
             A muKs.                        B muKs.                         C muKs.                        A yd C muKs.                    B yd C muKs.

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2020 - 22 )
          Answer : 5 )
          Blackbox Testing / ld, uxcqid mÍlaIdj
          Black box testing is a software testing technique that evaluates the functionality of an application without
          any knowledge of its internal code structure or implementation details.
          ld, uxcqid mÍlaIdj hkq fhÿul wNHka;r fla; jHqyh fyda l%shd;aul lsÍfï úia;r ms<sn| lsisÿ
          oekqulska f;drj tys l%shdldÍ;ajh we.hSug ,la lrk uDÿldx. mÍlaIK l%uhls'

          Whitebox Testing / iafõ; uxcqid mßlaIdj
          White box testing, also known as clear box or glass box testing, is a software testing methodology that
          focuses on examining the internal structures and workings of an application.
          iafõ; ukacqid mßlaIdj" meyeÈ,s fmÜáh fyda ùÿre fmÜá mÍlaIdj f,io ye¢kafõ" th fhÿul wNHka;r
          jHqyhka iy l%shdldÍ;ajh mÍlaId lsÍu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrk uDÿldx. mÍlaIK l%uhls'

          Unit Testing / tAll mÍlaIdj
          The smallest part of the application (often a function or a method) is tested in isolation.
          fhÿfï l=vdu fldgi ^fndfyda úg Y%s;hla fyda l%uhla& yqol,dj mÍlaId lrkq ,efí'

          Only the unit or module is tested, without considering how it interacts with other parts of the system.
          moaO;sfha wfkl=;a fldgia iuÕ wka;¾l%shd lrk wdldrh i,ld ne,Sfuka f;drj" tAllh fyda
          fudähq,h muKla mÍlaId lrkq ,efí'

          Integration Testing / tAldnoaO mÍlaIKh
          Tests the interaction between different units or components that have already passed unit testing.
          oekgu;a tAll mÍlaIK iu;a ù we;s úúO tAll fyda ixrpl w;r wka;¾l%shd mÍlaId lrhs'

          Instead of testing a single unit, test how different modules communicate and interact.
          ;ks tAllhla mÍlaId lsÍu fjkqjg" úúO fudähq, ikaksfõokh iy wka;¾l%shd lrk wdldrh mÍlaId lrhs'

          System Testing / moaO;s mÍlaIdj
          The entire system, including all modules and components, is tested.
          ish¨u fudähq, iy ixrpl we;=¿j iuia; moaO;shu mÍlaId lrkq ,efí'

          It tests the application in an environment similar to the production one and checks its behavior as a
          complete system. / th ksIamdokhg iudk mßirhl fhÿu mÍlaId lrk w;r iïmQ¾K moaO;shla
          f,i tys yeisÍu mÍlaId lrhs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -53-                                           #ictfromabc

    Acceptance Testing / m%;s.%yK mÍlaIKh
    Final phase of testing before the software goes live, performed from the end-user's perspective.
    uDÿldx.h iÔù ùug fmr wjidk mßlaIKfha wjidk wÈhr" wjidk mßYS,lhdf.a oDIaáfldaKfhka
    isÿ flf¾'

    Focuses on whether the system fulfills the real-world requirements of the users.

    The correct answer is, 5) B and C only
    ksjerÈ ms<s;=r kï" 5) B iy C muKs

    A. Black-box testing involves detailed checking of each line in the code.
       ld, uxcqid mÍlaIdjg fla;fhys iEu fma<shlau úis;rd;aulj mÍlaId lsÍu wvx.= fõ'
       This is incorrect. Black-box testing focuses on testing the functionality of the software without looking
       at the internal code. It evaluates whether the system produces expected outputs based on given inputs,
       without checking each line of code. Detailed code checking is associated with white-box testing.
       fuh jerÈhs' ld, uxcqid mÍlaIdj wNHka;r fla;h foi fkdn,d uDÿldx.fha l%shdldÍ;ajh mÍlaId
       lsÍu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs' tla tla fla; fma<s mÍlaId fkdlr" ,nd § we;s fhojqï u;
       mokïj moaO;sh wfmalaIs; m%;sodkhka ksmojkafkao hkak th we.hSug ,la lrhs' iúia;rd;aul
       fla; mÍlaIdj Yafõ; uxcqid mÍlaIdj iuÕ iïnkaO fõ'

    B. Unit-testing helps to uncover errors in the codes.
       tall mÍlaIdj (unit testing) fla;j, fodaI wkdjrKh lsÍug WmldÍ fõ'
       This is correct. Unit testing is designed to check individual components or modules of the software
       for errors and ensure they work as intended.
       fuh ksjerÈhs' tAll mÍlaIdj ie,iqï lr we;af;a uDÿldx.fha ;ks ixrpl fyda fudähq, fodaI
       i|yd mÍlaId lsÍug iy tAjd wfmalaIs; mßÈ l%shd lsÍu iy;sl lsÍug h'

    C. System testing should not be performed prior to unit-testing.
       tall mÍlaIdjg fmr moaO;s mßlaIdj isÿ fkdl< hq;= h'
       This is correct. System testing is performed after unit testing, integration testing, and other lower-level
       tests to ensure that the entire system works as expected. Unit testing is typically done early in the
       testing process, followed by integration and system testing.
       fuh ksjerÈhs' iuia; moaO;shu wfmalaIs; mßÈ l%shd;aul jk nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd tAll
       mÍlaIdj" tAldnoaO;d mÍlaIdj iy wfkl=;a my< uÜgfï mÍlaIKj,ska miqj moaO;s mÍlaIKh
       isÿ flfra' tAll mÍlaIdj idudkHfhka mÍlaIK l%shdj,sfha uq,a wjêfha§ isÿ lrkq ,nk w;r
       bka miqj tAldnoaO lsÍu iy moaO;s mÍlaIKh isÿ flf¾'

    Only Statements B and C are correct, so the answer is (5) B and C only.
    B iy C m%ldY muKla ksjerÈ neúka ms<s;=r (5) B iy C muKs'

 2. Which of the following indicate functional requirements?
    my; ljrla ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d (functional requirements) olajhs o?
    A. The users should be allowed to update their contact addresses and phone numbers.
       mßYS,lhkag ;ukaf.a ,smsk yd ÿrl;k wxl hdj;ald,Sk lsÍug wjia:dj ,nd Èh hq;= h'

       moaO;sh myiqfjka fjkia lsÍug yels úh hq;= h'

    1) A only.           2) B only.           3) C only.           4) A and C only.     5) All of the above.
       A muKs.              B muKs.              C muKs.              A yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

    Answer : 1 )

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -54-                                          #ictfromabc

    Non Functional Requirements / ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d
    Nonfunctional requirements specify the attributes and qualities that a system must have, rather than
    specific behaviors or functions it must perform. These requirements describe how well the system
    performs certain functions, rather than what it does. They are crucial for defining the overall quality,
    performance, and usability characteristics of the system, ensuring it meets the broader expectations and
    constraints beyond functional capabilities.
    ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d uÕska moaO;shla bgq l< hq;= ksYaÑ; yeisÍï fyda ld¾hhka fjkqjg ;sìh
    hq;= Wm,laIK iy Wm,laIK kshu lrhs' fuu wjYH;d moaO;sh th lrk foag jvd hï hï
    ld¾hhka bgq lrkafka flfiao hkak úia;r lrhs' moaO;sfha iuia; .=Kd;aulNdjh" ld¾h idOkh
    iy Wmfhda.S;d ,laIK ks¾jpkh lsÍu i|yd tAjd b;d jeo.;a fõ" th l%shdldÍ yelshdjkaf.ka
    Tíng mq¿,a wfmalaIdjka iy iSudjka imqrd,Su iy;sl lrhs'

    Functional Requirements / ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d
    Functional requirements describe what a system should do. They specify the functions, capabilities, and
    behaviors that the system must perform to satisfy its users and stakeholders. These requirements focus on
    the specific tasks and activities that the system needs to accomplish, often in response to inputs from users
    or other systems. Functional requirements are essential for defining the scope and purpose of the system's
    development and implementation.
    ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d moaO;shla l< hq;= foa úia;r lrhs' tys mßYS,lhska iy md¾Yjlrejka ;Dma;su;a
    lsÍu i|yd moaO;sh úiska bgq l< hq;= ld¾hhka" yelshdjka iy yeisÍï Tjqka kshu lrhs' fuu
    wjYH;d moaO;shg bgq lsÍug wjYH ksYaÑ; ld¾hhka iy l%shdldrlï flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs"
    fndfyda úg mßYS,lhska fyda fjk;a moaO;s j,ska ,efnk wdodk j,g m%;spdr olajhs' moaO;sfha
    ixj¾Okfha iy l%shd;aul lsÍfï úIh m:h iy wruqK ks¾jpkh lsÍu i|yd ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d
    w;HjYH fõ'

    The correct answer is, 1) A only
    ksjerÈ ms<s;=r kï" 1) A muKs

    A. The users should be allowed to update their contact addresses and phone numbers.
       mßYS,lhkag ;ukaf.a ,smsk yd ÿrl;k wxl hdj;ald,Sk lsÍug wjia:dj ,nd Èh hq;=h'
        This is a functional requirement because it specifies what the system should do, which is allowing
        users to update contact information. Functional requirements define specific behaviors or functions of
        the system.
        fuh ld¾h noaO wjYH;djhls" ukao th moaO;sh l< hq;= foa i|yka lrhs" tuÕska mßYS,lhskag
        iïnkaO;d f;dr;=re hdj;ald,Sk lsÍug bv i,ihs' ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d moaO;sfha ksYaÑ; yeisÍï
        fyda l%shdldrlï ks¾jpkh lrhs'

       ´kEu mßYS,l b,a,Sulgu 2 ms l ld,hla ;=< m%;spdr oelaúh hq;=h'
        This is a non-functional requirement as it describes a performance aspect of the system (response time)

    Software Process Model / uDÿldx. l%shdhk wdlD;sh
    A software process model is a structured approach that defines the sequence of activities, tasks, and
    deliverables needed to develop a software product. It serves as a framework for planning, organizing, and
    controlling the software development process. Different process models suit different types of projects,
    depending on factors like project size, complexity, and customer requirements.
    uDÿldx. l%shdhk wdlD;shla hkq uDÿldx. ksIamdokhla ixj¾Okh lsÍug wjYH l%shdldrlï" ld¾hhka
    iy fnodyeÍï wkqms<sfj, ks¾jpkh lrk jHqy.; m%fõYhls' th uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok l%shdj,sh
    ie,iqï lsÍu" ixúOdkh lsÍu iy md,kh lsÍu i|yd rduqjla f,i fiajh lrhs' jHdmD;s m%udKh"
    ixlS¾K;ajh iy mdßfNda.sl wjYH;d jeks idOl u; mokïj úúO l%shdj,s wdlD;s úúO wdldrfha
    jHdmD;sj,g .e,fma'

    Agile model / iqp,H wdlD;sh
    The Agile Model is a software development methodology focused on delivering small, functional parts of
    the product in iterative cycles called "sprints."
    iqp,H wdlD;sh hkq iam%skaÜ f,i ye¢kafjk mqkrdj¾;k pl%j, ksIamdokfha l=vd" l%shdldÍ fldgia
    fnod yeÍu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrk uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok l%ufõohls'

    It emphasizes collaboration, flexibility, and quick delivery, allowing teams to adapt to changing
    requirements throughout the project.
    th iyfhda.S;dj" kuHYS,S nj iy blauka fnodyeÍu wjOdrKh lrhs" jHdmD;sh mqrdjgu fjkiajk
    wjYH;dj,g wkqj¾;kh ùug lKavdhïj,g bv i,ihs'

    Prototype Model / uQ,dlD;s wdlD;sh
    The Prototype Model is a software development methodology where a prototype or early model of the
    final product is built, tested, and refined based on user feedback before creating the actual system.
    uQ,dlD;s wdlD;sh hkq uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok l%ufõohla jk w;r tys§ wjidk ksIamdokfha uQ,dlD;shla
    fyda uq,a wdlD;shla ienE moaO;sh ks¾udKh lsÍug fmr mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIK u; mokïj f.dvk.d"
    mÍlaId lr" iy msßisÿ lrkq ,efí'

    It focuses on capturing user requirements by presenting them with a working, though incomplete, version
    of the product.
    th wiïmQ¾K jqj;a" ksIamdokfha jev lrk wkqjdohla f.dvke.Sfuka mßYS,l wjYH;d .%yKh lr
    .ekSu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs'

    The Prototype Model is particularly effective when requirements are unclear or subject to change, enabling
    early validation through constant user interaction.

    j,x.=lrKh il%Sh lrñka uQ,dlD;s wdlD;sh úfYaIfhka M,odhS fõ'

 4. Which of the following statements on Data Flow Diagrams (DFD) is incorrect?
    o;a; .e,Sï igyka (Data Flow Diagram-DFD) ms<sn| my; ljr j.ka;sh idjoH fõ o?

     1) Context diagram is a DFD with the highest level of abstraction.
        ikao¾N igyk hkq by<u uÜgfï úhqla;lrKh (abstraction) we;s o;a; .e,Sï igykls'
     2) All data stores in a system must be represented in the context diagram.
        moaO;sfhys we;s ish¨u o;a; .nvd (data stores) ikao¾N igyfkys ksrEmKh l< hq;= h'
     3) Data flows are used to link the other components in DFDs.
        DFD ys wfkl=;a ixrpl iïnkaO lsÍu i|yd o;a; .e,Sï Ndú; flf¾'
     4) Elementary processes are not decomposed further.
        uQ,sl l%shdhk (elementary processes) ;jÿrg;a úLKavkh (decompose) fkdflf¾'
     5) External entities in DFDs act as sources or recipients of data.
        DFD ys ndysr N+;d¾: (external entities), o;a; m%Nj (sources) fyda o;a; ,nd.kakd (recepients)
        wx. f,i fyda l%shd flf¾'

                                                                                                    ( 2020 - 25 )
     Answer : 2 )
     A Data Flow Diagram (DFD) is a visual representation of how data flows within a system.
     o;a; .e,Sï igykla (DFD) hkq moaO;shla ;=< o;a; .,d hk wdldrh ms<sn| oDYH ksrEmKhls'

     Let’s go through the statements given.
     § we;s m%ldY yryd huq'

     Statement 1/ 1 m%ldYkh
     Correct. A context diagram provides an overview of the system, showing external entities and major data
     flows but no detailed processes or data stores.
     ksjerÈ fõ' ikao¾Nh rEm igykla uÕska moaO;sh ms<sn| o< úYaf,aIKhla imhhs" ndysr N+;d¾:
     iy m%Odk o;a; m%jdyhka fmkajk kuq;a iúia;rd;aul l%shdj,s fyda o;a; .nvd fkdue;'

     Statement 2/ 2 m%ldYkh
     Incorrect. Context diagrams only show external entities and the primary data flows between the system
     and those entities. Data stores are not included in the context diagram.
     jerÈ fõ' ikao¾N rEmigyka fmkajkafka ndysr N+;d¾: muKla jk w;r m%d:ñl o;a; moaO;sh iy
     tu N+;d¾: w;r .,d hhs' o;a; .nvd ikao¾Nh rEm igyfkys we;=<;a fkdfõ'

     Statement 3/ 3 m%ldYkh
     Correct. Data flows represent the movement of data between processes, data stores, and external entities
     in a DFD.
     ksjerÈ fõ' o;a; m%jdyhka DFD tll l%shdj,s" o;a; .nvd iy ndysr N+;d¾: w;r o;a; p,kh
     ksfhdackh lrhs'

     Statement 4/ 4 m%ldYkh
     Correct. Elementary processes are the most detailed level of a process and cannot be broken down further.
     ksjerÈ fõ' m%d:ñl l%shdj,s hkq l%shdj,shl jvd;a iúia;rd;aul uÜgu jk w;r th ;jÿrg;a ì¢h
     fkdyel'

     Statement 5/ 5 m%ldYkh
     Correct. External entities represent the system's interaction points, either providing data to the system or
     receiving data from it.
     ksjerÈ fõ' ndysr N+;d¾: uÕska moaO;sfha moaO;shg o;a; iemhSu fyda thska o;a; ,nd .ekSu hk
     wka;¾l%shd ,laIH ksfhdackh lrhs'

     Therefore, the incorrect statement about Data Flow Diagrams (DFD) is, 2).
     tneúka" o;a; .e,Sï igykla (DFD) ms<sn| jerÈ m%ldYh jkafka" 2) fõ'

    Typical Order of Activities in Database System Development
    o;a; iuqod moaO;s ixj¾Okfha idudkH l%shdldrlï wkqms<sfj,

    1. Draw the DFD (Data Flow Diagram) / DFD rEm igyka w¢kak (o;a; m%jdy igyk)
        The DFD is typically drawn first to represent the flow of data within the system. It helps visualize
        how data moves between different processes and data stores.
        moaO;sh ;=< o;a; .,dhdu ksrEmKh lsÍu i|yd DFD idudkHfhka m<uqj w¢kq ,efí' th úúO
        l%shdj,s iy o;a; .nvd w;r o;a; p,kh jk wdldrh oDYHudk lsÍug WmldÍ fõ'

    2. Draw the ER Diagrams (Entity-Relationship Diagrams) / ER rEm igyka w¢kak
        ER diagrams are created next to model the database structure. They help in identifying entities,
        relationships, and attributes that will form the foundation of the database design.
        o;a; iuqod jHqyh wdo¾Yhg .ekSug ER rEm igyka idokq ,efí' o;a; iuqod ie,iqfï moku
        jk N=;d¾:" iïnkaO;d iy Wm,laIK y÷kd .ekSug tAjd Wmldr lrhs'

    3. Design the Database / o;a; iuqodh ie,iqï lrkak
        After understanding the data flow and the entities involved, the database is designed. This involves
        creating tables, defining keys, and setting up relationships based on the ER diagram.
        o;a; m%jdyh iy thg iïnkaO N=;d¾: wjfndaO lr .ekSfuka miqj" o;a; iuqodh ks¾udKh lr
        we;' fuhg j.= iE§u" h;=re ks¾jpkh lsÍu iy ER rEm igyk u; mokïj iïnkaO;d
        msysgqùu we;=<;a fõ'

    4. Write the Pseudo-code / jHdc fla;h ,shkak
        Pseudo-code is written to outline the logic and structure of the code before actual implementation. It
        serves as a blueprint for coding.
        jHdc fla;h ,shd we;af;a i;H l%shdjg kexùug fmr fla;fha ;¾lkh iy jHqyh f.kyer
        oelaùug h' th fla;SlrKh i|yd ie,eiaula f,i fiajh lrhs'

    5. Do the Coding / fla;kh lrkak

        we;'

    Option 4)

    • Draw the ER diagrams / ER rEm igyka w¢kak

    Let's analyze each statement to determine which one is correct about software deployment.

    úYaf,aIKh lruq'

    Statement / m%ldYh 01
    Direct deployment has the highest risk of complete failure but may be the only suitable method for some
    cases.
    iDcq (direct) ia:dmkh mQ¾K ì|jeàula isÿùfï jeäu wjodkula iys; jqj o iuyr wjia:d i|yd
    th tlu iqÿiq l%uh fõ'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -69-                                           #ictfromabc

    Correct. Direct deployment, also known as "big bang" deployment, involves switching from the old
    system to the new system in one step. It has the highest risk because there is no fallback if the new system
    fails, but it might be necessary in certain situations where parallel or phased approaches are not feasible.
    ksjerÈhs' iDcq ia:dmkh" "big bang" fhoùu f,io ye¢kafõ" th mer” moaO;sfhka kj moaO;shg
    tla mshjrlska udreùu we;=<;a fõ' kj moaO;sh wiu;a jqjfyd;a miqnEula fkdue;s ksid thg by<u
    wjodkula we;" kuq;a iudka;r fyda wÈhr m%fõYhka l< fkdyels we;eï wjia:djkays§ th wjYH
    úh yelsh'

    Statement / m%ldYh 02
    Direct deployment is the most expensive and offers the slowest learning to the users.
    iDcq ia:dmkh jeäu úhoï iys; l%uh jk w;r mßYS,lhkag fyñka moaO;sh .ek bf.kSug
    bvfohs'

    Incorrect. Direct deployment is often less expensive in the short term because it doesn't require running
    two systems in parallel, but it carries high risk. The speed of user learning depends on training and support,
    not on the deployment method itself.
    jerÈhs' iDcq ia:dmkh fndfyda úg flá ld,Skj ñf,ka wvq jkafka thg iudka;rj moaO;s folla
    l%shd;aul lsÍu wjYH fkdjk kuq;a th by< wjodkula orhs' mßYS,l bf.kSfï fõ.h r|d
    mj;skafka mqyqKqj iy iydh u; ñi fhoùfï l%uh u; fkdfõ'
    Statement / m%ldYh 03
    Parallel deployment is the least expensive deployment option.
    iudka;r (parellel) ia:dmkh wvqu úhoula iys; ia:dmk l%uhhs'

    Incorrect. Parallel deployment is often more expensive because it involves running both the old and new
    systems simultaneously, which requires more resources and effort.
    jerÈhs' iudka;r ia:dmkh fndfyda úg jvd ñ, wêl jkafka thg mer” iy kj moaO;s folu
    tljr l%shd;aul lsÍu iïnkaO jk ksid" jeä iïm;a yd jEhula wjYH fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh 04
    Phased deployment does not provide the freedom for the relevant organization to make any needed
    adjustments to the system.
    wjê (phased) ia:dmkh moaO;shg wjYH hï hï fjkialï lsÍug wod< ixúOdkhg ksoyi fkdfoa'

    Incorrect. Phased deployment, where the new system is implemented in stages, allows for adjustments
    and improvements after each phase, providing flexibility.
    jerÈhs' wÈhr jYfhka ia:dmkh kj moaO;sh wÈhr jYfhka l%shd;aul jk w;r" tla tla wÈhfrka
    miq .e,mSï iy jeäÈhqKq lsÍï i|yd kuHYS,S njla ,nd foa'

    Statement / m%ldYh 05
    Pilot deployment always rolls out the new system to a test user group larger than 50% of the users.
    kshdul (pilot) ia:dmkh yeuúgu mßYs,lhkaf.ka 50% lg jeä mßYS,l lKavdhulg kj moaO;sh
    mÍlaIdj i|yd fhduq lrhs'

    Incorrect. Pilot deployment typically involves rolling out the system to a small group of users (often less
    than 50%) to test and validate it before full deployment.
    jerÈhs' kshuq ia:dmkh idudkHfhka l=vd mßYS,l lKavdhulg ^fndfyda úg 50] g jvd wvq& moaO;sh

          System Installation Methods / moaO;s ia:dmk l%u

          System installation methods refer to the various approaches and strategies used to deploy a new system or
          software in an organization.
          moaO;s ia:dmk l%u hkq wdh;khl kj moaO;shla fyda uDÿldx.hla fhoùug Ndú;d lrk úúO

          Direct Implimentation / iDcq ia:dmkh

          The old system is completely replaced by the new system in one single operation.
          tla fufyhqulska merKs moaO;sh iïmQ¾Kfhkau kj moaO;sh u.ska m%;sia:dmkh fõ'

          This method is often used when the new system must be implemented by a specific date, such as the
          beginning of a fiscal year. / uQ,H j¾Ihl wdrïNh jeks ksYaÑ; Èkhlska kj l%uh l%shd;aul l< hq;=
          úg fuu l%uh fndfyda úg Ndú;d fõ'

     Pilot Implimentation kshuq ia:dmkh

     before full-scale deployment. Feedback from the pilot group is used to make adjustments before wider
     implementation.

     Information systems (IS) are organized systems designed to collect, process, store, and disseminate
     information. They combine technology, people, and processes to manage and analyze data, enabling

     vary in scope and function, from simple applications managing day-to-day activities to complex systems
     supporting enterprise-wide functions.

     System Development Life Cycle
     moaO;s ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%h
     The System Development Life Cycle (SDLC) is a framework used to guide the process of developing
     information systems and software applications. There are various SDLC models, each with its own
     approach to organizing and managing these phases.
     moaO;s ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%h (SDLC) hkq f;dr;=re moaO;s iy uDÿldx. fhÿï ixj¾Okh lsÍfï

     ixúOdkh lsÍu iy l<ukdlrKh lsÍu i|yd ;ukaf.au m%fõYhla we;'

     Waterfall Model Èh we,s wdlD;sh
     The Waterfall Model is a linear and sequential approach to software development. Each phase must be
     completed before the next one begins, and there is little to no overlap between phases.
     Èh we,s wdlD;sh hkq uDÿldx. ixj¾Okh i|yd f¾Çh iy wkql%ñl m%fõYhls' iEu wÈhrlau B<Õ

     4. Integration and Testing tAldnoaO lsÍu iy mÍlaId lsÍu
     5. Deployment fhoùu
     6. Maintenance kv;a;= lsÍu

      Answer : 2 )
      Functional Requirements ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d
      Functional requirements specify the functions, capabilities, and behaviors that the system must perform to
      satisfy its users and stakeholders. These requirements focus on the specific tasks and activities that the
      system needs to accomplish, often in response to inputs from users or other systems.
      ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d moaO;shla mßYS,lhska iy md¾Yjlrejka ;Dma;su;a lsÍu i|yd moaO;sh úiska
      bgq l< hq;= ld¾hhka" yelshdjka iy yeisÍï Tjqka kshu lrhs' fuu wjYH;d moaO;shg bgq lsÍug
      wjYH ksYaÑ; ld¾hhka iy l%shdldrlï flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs" fndfyda úg mßYS,lhska fyda
      fjk;a moaO;s j,ska ,efnk wdodk j,g m%;spdr olajhs'

      Non Functional Requirements ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d
      Non functional requirements specify the attributes and qualities that a system must have, rather than
      specific behaviors or functions it must perform. These requirements describe how well the system
      performs certain functions, rather than what it does. They are crucial for defining the overall quality,
      performance, and usability characteristics of the system.
      ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d uÕska moaO;shla bgq l< hq;= ksYaÑ; yeisÍï fyda ld¾hhka fjkqjg ;sìh
      hq;= .=Kdx. kshu lrhs' fuu wjYH;d moaO;sh th lrk foag jvd hï hï ld¾hhka bgq lrkafka

       Let's analyze each option based on the general rules of data flow diagrams:
       o;a; m%jdy rEm igyka j, idudkH Í;s u; mokïj tla tla úl,amh úYaf,aIKh lruq'

       1. Data flow diagrams must have properly directed flows:

            Data flows from external entities to processes, from processes to data stores, and from data stores

            A (external entity): Source or destination of data outside the system.
            A ^ndysr N+;d¾:h&( moaO;sfhka msg; o;a;j, uQ,dY%h fyda .ukdka;h'
            B (process): Transforms incoming data flows into outgoing data flows.
            B ^l%shdj,sh&( tk o;a; m%jdy msg;g hk o;a; m%jdy njg mßj¾;kh lrhs'
            C (data store): Stores data for the system; only processes can interact directly with data stores.
            C ^o;a; .nvdj&( moaO;sh i|yd o;a; .nvd lrhs; o;a; .nvd iuÕ iDcqj wka;¾ l%shd l<
            yelafla l%shdj,Skag muKs'

       oeka" wms tla tla o;a; .e,Sï rEmigyka we.hSug ,la lruq'

       1) Data flows from external entity A to process B and then to data store C. This aligns with the rules,
          where an external entity sends data to a process, which then can store it in a data store.
          o;a; A isg B ieliSug iy C o;a; .nvdj fj; .,d hhs' fuh kS;sÍ;s iuÕ iumd; fõ" tys§
          ndysr N+;d¾:hla l%shdj,shlg o;a; hjk w;r" th o;a; .nvdjl .nvd l< yel'

       2) Process B sends data to external entity A, and then A sends data to data store C. This is incorrect

     The question lists five statements about software testing and asks which one is correct. Let's analyze each
     statement: / m%Yakh uDÿldx. mÍlaIdj ms<sn| m%ldY myla ,ehsia;=.; lr we;s w;r l=uk tl ksjerÈ
     oehs tla tla m%ldYh f.k úYaf,aIKh lruq'

     1. Integration testing is usually carried out before unit testing.
        taldnoaO (integration) mÍlaIdj idudkHfhka tall (unit) mÍlaIdjg fmr isÿ flf¾'
         This is incorrect. Unit testing is done first, where individual units or components of a software are
         tested. Integration testing follows unit testing and focuses on the integration of these units to ensure
         they work together as expected.
         fuh jerÈhs' uDÿldx.hl ;ks tAll fyda ixrpl mÍlaId flfrk ;ekska tAll (unit) mÍlaIKh

         wjOdkh fhduq lrhs'

     2. Black-box testing techniques are usually used in acceptance testing.
        ld,uxcqid (black-box) mßlaIdfõ Ys,amSh l%u idudkHfhka m%;s.%yK mßlaIdfõ§ Ndú; fõ'
        This statement is correct. Black-box testing involves testing the software without any knowledge of
        the internal workings of the application. This technique is typically used in acceptance testing where
        the focus is on the functionality according to the requirements, rather than the internal system logic.
        fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' ld,uxcqid (black-box) mÍlaIdj hkq fhÿfï wNHka;r l%shdldß;ajh ms<sn|

     3. White box testing examines the behavior of a software based only on the inputs to a system.
        Yafõ; uxcqid (white-box) mÍlaIdfõ § uDÿldx.hl yeisÍu" moaO;shg ,nd fok wdodk u; muKla
        mokïj mÍlaId flf¾'
        This statement is incorrect. White box testing (also known as clear box testing or glass box testing)
        involves looking at the structure and logic of the code. Testers need knowledge of the internal workings
        of the software, as they test specific internal coding and infrastructure.
        fuu m%ldYh jerÈhs' Yafõ; uxcqid (white-box) mÍlaIdj ^meyeÈ,s uxcqid mÍlaIdj fyda ùÿre
        uxcqid mÍlaIdj f,io ye¢kafõ& fla;fha jHqyh iy ;¾lkh ne,Su we;=<;a fõ' úfYaIs;

          System Installation Methods - moaO;s ia:dmk l%u
          System installation methods refer to the various approaches and strategies used to deploy a new system or
          software in an organization. Choosing the right installation method is crucial for minimizing disruption,
          ensuring smooth operation, and achieving a successful implementation.
          moaO;s ia:dmk l%u hkq wdh;khl kj moaO;shla fyda uDÿldx.hla fhoùug Ndú;d lrk úúO
          m%fõYhka iy Wmdh ud¾.hs' ndOd lsÍï wju lsÍu" iqugj l%shd;aul ùu iy;sl lsÍu iy id¾:l
          l%shdjla idlaId;a lr .ekSu i|yd ksjerÈ ia:dmk l%uh f;dard .ekSu b;d jeo.;a fõ'
          Direct Implimentation - iDcq ia:dmkh
          The old system is completely replaced by the new system in one single operation.This method is often
          used when the new system must be implemented by a specific date, such as the beginning of a fiscal year.
          tla fufyhqulska merKs moaO;sh iïmQ¾Kfhkau kj moaO;sh u.ska m%;sia:dmkh fõ' uQ,H j¾Ihl
          wdrïNh jeks ksYaÑ; Èkhlska kj l%uh l%shd;aul l< hq;= úg fuu l%uh fndfyda úg Ndú;d fõ'

          Advantages - jdis
          •     Quickest method as the transition happens in one step.
                ixl%dka;sh tla mshjrlska isÿjk neúka fõ.j;au l%uh'
          •     Less expensive in terms of maintaining only one system.
                tla moaO;shla muKla mj;ajdf.k hdu iïnkaOfhka wvq úhoï'

          Disadvantages - wjdis
          •     High risk due to immediate switch-over.
                laIKslj udre ùu fya;=fjka by< wjodku'
          •     Any problems with the new system can cause major disruptions.
                kj moaO;sh iuÕ we;s ´kEu .eg¿jla úYd, ndOd we;s úh yel'

    Parallel Implimentation - iudka;r ia:dmkh
    Both the old and new systems run simultaneously for a period. Users gradually transition from the old
    system to the new one while both systems are operational.

    Non Functional Requirements - ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d
    Non-functional requirements specify the attributes and qualities that a system must have, rather than
    specific behaviors or functions it must perform. These requirements describe how well the system
    performs certain functions, rather than what it does. They are crucial for defining the overall quality,
    performance, and usability characteristics of the system, ensuring it meets the broader expectations and
    constraints beyond functional capabilities.
    ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d uÕska moaO;shla bgq l< hq;= ksYaÑ; yeisÍï fyda ld¾hhka fjkqjg ;sìh
    hq;= .=Kdx. iy .=Kdx. kshu lrhs' fuu wjYH;d moaO;sh th lrk foag jvd hï hï ld¾hhka
    bgq lrkafka flfiao hkak úia;r lrhs' moaO;sfha iuia; .=Kd;aulNdjh" ld¾h idOkh iy Wmfhda.S;d
    ,laIK ks¾jpkh lsÍu i|yd tAjd b;d jeo.;a fõ" th l%shdldÍ yelshdjkaf.ka Tíng mq¿,a
    wfmalaIdjka iy iSudjka imqrd,Su iy;sl lrhs'

    Functional Requirements - ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d
    Functional requirements describe what a system should do. They specify the functions, capabilities, and
    behaviors that the system must perform to satisfy its users and stakeholders. These requirements focus on
    the specific tasks and activities that the system needs to accomplish, often in response to inputs from users
    or other systems. Functional requirements are essential for defining the scope and purpose of the system's
    development and implementation.
    ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d moaO;shla l< hq;= foa úia;r lrhs' tys mßYS,lhska iy md¾Yjlrejka ;Dma;su;a
    lsÍu i|yd moaO;sh úiska bgq l< hq;= ld¾hhka" yelshdjka iy yeisÍï Tjqka kshu lrhs' fuu

    Acceptance Testing - m%;s.%yK mÍlaIdj

    Integration Testing - taldnoaO mÍlaIdj

    tAldnoaO ixrpl ksjerÈj tlg jev lrk nj iy;sl lrhs'

    Parallel Testing - iudka;r mÍlaIdj

    system operates correctly.

    moaO;sh iuÕ kj moaO;sh Odjkh lsÍu we;=<;a fõ'

    Ndú;d lrhs'

 6. Which of the following is incorrect about the waterfall model of software development?
    uDÿldx. f.dvke.Sug wod< Èhwe,s (waterfall) wdlD;sh iïnkaOfhka wi;H jkafka my; ljrla o?
    1) It allows developers to collect and implement requirements throughout the project.
       ixj¾Olhkag jHdmD;sh mqrdjgu wjYH;d tl;= lsÍug;a" l%shd;aul lsÍug;a th bv fohs'
    2) It is not an iterative model.
       th mqkrdj¾;S (iterative) wdlD;shla fkdfõ'
    3) It is suitable for software with well defined requirements.
       ukdj ks¾Kh lrk ,o wjYH;d iys; uDÿldx. i|yd th iqÿiq fõ'
    4) It is easy to estimate the resources needed for a project.
       jHdmD;shla i|yd wjYH iïm;a weia;fïka;= lsÍu myiq h'
    5) No working product is available until the latter stages of the project.
       jHdmD;sfha wjika wjê jk f;la" jevlrk ksIamdÈ;hla (working product) ,nd.; fkdyel'

                                                                                                 ( 2023 - 29 )
    Answer : 1 )
    1) It allows developers to collect and implement requirements throughout the project.
       ixj¾Olhkag jHdmD;sh mqrdjgu wjYH;d tl;= lsÍug;a" l%shd;aul lsÍug;a th bv fohs'
        Incorrect - jerÈhs
        The waterfall model is a linear and sequential approach to software development. In this model,
        requirements are collected at the beginning of the project during the "Requirements Analysis" phase
        and are expected to remain fixed throughout the project. Once the requirements are set, the project
        progresses through the subsequent stages (design, implementation, testing, etc.) without revisiting or
        modifying the earlier stages. Therefore, this statement is incorrect as the waterfall model does not
        allow for ongoing requirements collection and implementation.

        any detailed design or analysis, it’s important to ensure the project can actually be completed given
        the available resources and constraints.

 4. Which of the following is not true regarding the prototyping technique used in system development?
    moaO;s ixj¾Okfha§ Ndú;d jk uq,dlD;slrKh (prototyping) iïnkaOfhka my; ljrla wi;H o@

       moaO;s mÍlaId (system testing) wÈhf¾§ uQ,dlD;s" mßYS,lhkaf.ka wkqu; úh hq;=h'

    5) To get the benefits of prototyping, user feedback on the prototypes is extremely important.
       uq,dlD;slrKfha jdis ,nd.ekSug uq,dlD;s ms<sn| mßYS,l m%;spdr w;sYhska jeo.;a fõ

                                                                                                  ( 2024 - 27 )
    Answer : 1 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Prototypes are primarily used in the early stages of system development to gather user feedback and refine
    requirements. The statement "Prototypes need to be approved by the users during the 'system testing'
    phase" is incorrect because prototyping focuses on creating initial models for user interaction before full
    development begins. System testing occurs much later in the development lifecycle, after the system is
    fully implemented, and is meant to validate the complete product rather than prototypes.
    mßYS,l m%;sfmdaIK tla/ia lsÍu iy wjYH;d msßmyÿ lsÍu i|yd uQ,dlD;s uQ,sl jYfhka moaO;s
    ixj¾Okfha uq,a wjêfha§ Ndú;d fõ' "moaO;s mÍlaId lsÍfï wÈhf¾ § mßYS,lhska úiska uQ,dlD;slKh
    wkqu; l< hq;=h" hk m%ldYh jerÈh" ukao uQ,dlD;slrKh mQ¾K ixj¾Okh wdrïN ùug fmr
    mßYS,l wka;¾l%shd i|yd uQ,sl wdlD;s ks¾udKh lsÍu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrk neúks' moaO;sh
    iïmQ¾Kfhka l%shd;aul lsÍfuka miqj" ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%fha fndfyda miqld,Skj moaO;s mÍlaIdj isÿ
    jk w;r" uQ,dlD;slrK j,g jvd iïmQ¾K ksIamdokh j,x.= lsÍug woyia flf¾'

    Answer: 3)

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Statement / m%ldYh A
    White-box testing (also known as structural or glass-box testing) involves testing the internal structures,
    logic, and implementation of the software. Testers must have knowledge of the code and its internal
    workings.
    Yafõ; uxcqid mÍlaIdj ^jHqyd;aul fyda ùÿre fmÜá mÍlaIdj f,io ye¢kafõ& uDÿldx.fha wNHka;r

    This statement is true.
    fuu m%ldYh i;Hhls'

    Statement / m%ldYh B
    Unit testing is the process of testing individual components or modules of a program in isolation. It is
    usually performed at the earliest stages of testing, before integration testing and system testing.
    tAll mÍlaIdj hkq jevigykl ;ks ixrpl fyda fudähq, yqol,dj mÍlaId lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs' th
    idudkHfhka isÿ lrkq ,nkafka mÍlaIKj, uq,a wjêfha§" tAldnoaO;d mÍlaIdjg iy moaO;s mÍlaIdjg
    fmrh'

    System testing, on the other hand, is a high-level test performed after the entire system is integrated to
    ensure it meets the requirements.
    wfkla w;g" moaO;s mÍlaIdj hkq wjYH;d imqrd,k nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd iïmQ¾K moaO;shu
    tAldnoaO lsÍfuka miqj isÿ lrkq ,nk by< uÜgfï mÍlaIKhls'

    Therefore, unit tests are performed before system tests, not after them.

    This statement is false.
